## 2026-03-16 — Kaizen

### Reflection

“The former things shall not be remembered . . .” – There are only three types of people in the world. First, people who make things happen. Second, people who watch things happen. And third, people who wake up suddenly and say, “What happened?!” Which of the three types are you? My prayer is that we lessen being the second and eliminate as much as possible being the third. Why? Because we always have a choice. We are not destined to go blindly unaware of where we’re headed and what we’re doing with our lives. We all can choose to be better. Kaizen means “continuous improvement.” As an organizational development practitioner and a strengths coach, I believe we’re all called to grow and progress­ — individually and collectively. Change is our choice. What you’re not changing, you’re choosing. I joined a program with that title to get into shape and improve my fitness. It helped me beyond my physical health and made me even more determined to better myself in all aspects of life. Jesus calls us to a new life in all aspects. He came so we may be set free and live life to the full. Maybe it’s time you consider His offer. Jonathan Yogawin 

### Saint of the Day

Saint Dentlin

## 2026-03-17 — Jesus, The Life Coach

### Reflection

When Jesus saw him lying there and knew that he had been ill for a long time, he said to him, “Do you want to be well?” – Jesus is the ultimate Life Coach. His query to the paralyzed man brought him healing. I, too, had a coaching experience with the Lord. During one quiet time in prayer, my Life Coach “care-fronted” me with these pivotal questions: 1) What is the best thing to do? 2) How do you glorify the Father? 3) What is fruitfulness? 4) What’s the best way to become? I was reading and it addressed all four questions. The best thing to do is to glorify the Father. To glorify the Father is to be spiritually fruitful. Fruitfulness is to become disciples—followers of Jesus. The best way to become is to keep growing as Jesus’ disciples or, better yet, a disciple-making disciple. In just one verse, I know what kind of life I must live through all my life roles—as disciple, family man, builder, professional, and Each day, I should glorify the Father. I am to live out spiritual fruitfulness in character (cf. ). I should grow in discipleship and make disciple-making disciples. Obet Cabrillas 

### Prayer

Lord, bless me so I can do what You want me to do, go where You want me to go, and give what You want me to give, so that I can be what You want me to be. Saint Patrick, bishop of Ireland,

## 2026-03-18 — His Justice Is Mercy

### Reflection

The Lord is just in all his ways, merciful in all his works. – My friend Sheryl Coronel posted this after former president Rodrigo Duterte was arrested by the International Criminal Court: “It is proof that God’s love does not discriminate. . . . It is also proof that God loves even the unjust that Duterte’s freedom has been taken from him. Perhaps in the silence of his quarters, stripped of his power and influence, he can finally hear the voice of God. Here is a man determined to go to hell. But Jesus did not come to condemn but to save. This is proof that He has intervened in the life of Duterte, offering to him a chance to reflect, to repent, to come back to God.” You may or may not agree with my friend’s stand depending on your political bias. But her post made me realize that what we sometimes think as punishment could actually be God’s mercy at work. God’s nature is not to punish. If it was, then He would have left us to our sins and not sent His Only Son. God’s nature is to redeem. He wants to save us. So even His justice is actually His mercy to set us on the right path. Rissa Kawpeng 

### Prayer

God’s justice and mercy are two sides of the same coin. What you think is an unfortunate incident in your life could very well be God’s intervention to bring about results beyond what you ever wished for. Father, have mercy on me, a sinner. Amen.

## 2026-03-19 — Joseph’S Love Story

### Reflection

Joseph her husband, since he was a righteous 2 man, yet unwilling to expose her to shame, decided to divorce her quietly. – (or ) Why don’t we read a lot about Joseph’s deep and genuine love for Spouse of the Blessed Mary? Maybe we tend to see Joseph and Mary as purely Bible characters who had to get married to each other because “God told them so.” It was obedience to God, and “nothing personal.” Hmmm, but I don’t think so. Because Mary was pregnant before the marriage was consummated, she could be stoned to death, as prescribed by Levitical laws. But Joseph was not just a righteous man; he was deeply in love with Mary. To “divorce her quietly” meant he might be the one to be accused of impregnating Mary and escaping his responsibility. To save Mary from public shame, he’d be the one to absorb the shame instead. He was ready to make the sacrifice for the one he loved. Then the angel intervened and set everything right! Is your family or relationship in danger of crumbling just like Joseph’s? As long as someone is willing to make the sacrifice for love, there’s hope. That someone might be you. Then an angel comes to set everything right. Jon Escoto

Blessed Virgin Mary

## 2026-03-20 — Star Wars Kid

Let us put him to the test that we may have Wisdom 2:1, 12-22 proof of his gentleness and try his patience. – Wisdom 2:19, NRSVCE Quebec, 2003. Ghyslain Raza, 15, filmed himself pretending to be Darth Maul, a Star Wars villain. He innocently goofed around as he swung a stick and improvised his cool lightsaber choreography. But someone found his recorded performance and uploaded it to the web. It got almost a billion views at a time when YouTube didn’t exist yet. People made fun of Raza by harassing him in school. Online, strangers wished him dead. Raza was able to overcome this traumatic experience. He became a lawyer and publicly spoke on the negative effects of social media as an early victim of cyberbullying. When I checked for recent news on him, I saw posts ridiculing Raza’s receding hairline and weight, that he was a crybaby, etc. Sadly, haters never stop. We thoughtlessly say or do hurtful things to others. But doing it with intentions to bully is something else. Let the Spirit of God guide our conscience to make the right actions. Let’s try to put others first before ourselves. For the Lord said, “What you do to one of the least of your brethren, you do unto Me.” Gina J. Verdolaga 

### Prayer

Lord, grant me a generous heart. May I always be a source of comfort to my brethren. St. Maria Josefa Sancho de Guerra,

## 2026-03-21 — Happy World Down

### Reflection

“You are not from Galilee also, are you?”” – Today is World Down Syndrome Day and I honor my precious granddaughter, Pana, who has Down syndrome. March 21, the third month of the year, was selected to signify the uniqueness of the triplication (trisomy) of the 21st chromosome that causes the condition. The extra chromosome can cause differences in learning, physical features, and overall growth. In our key verse, the question reflects how people often judge others based on background or appearance. But Jesus taught us to look beyond external differences and embrace the inherent worth of every person. As we commemorate His passion, death, and resurrection this week, let’s remember that He rose from the dead not for a chosen few but for all of us. Pana just turned five. Today I celebrate her achievements and abilities. God makes no mistakes. He created Pana as a gift to our family. We know that He will provide her with the choicest blessings necessary to live a happy and fulfilling life. Mari SisonGarcia 

### Prayer

Lord, help me avoid categorizing and discriminating against others. Give me the grace and the humility to treat everyone equally like Jesus did. Blessed Maria Candida of the Eucharist,

## 2026-03-22 — When God Seems Late

### Reflection

Jesus wept. – Have you felt like God was taking too long to answer? Maybe you prayed for healing, a breakthrough, Fifth Sunday of Lent or something to change, but nothing happened—at least not when you wanted it to. That’s how Mary and Martha felt. Their brother, Lazarus, was dying, but Jesus didn’t come right away. When He arrived, Lazarus had already been in the tomb for four days. Imagine their grief and disappointment. “Lord, if You had been here, my brother would not have died,” Martha said. How many times have we thought the same thing? “God, if You had just stepped in sooner, things wouldn’t have fallen But Jesus wasn’t late. He was right on time for something greater. Instead of healing a sick man, He raised a dead man. Instead of preventing their grief, He showed them His power over it. Jesus wept—not because He was powerless—but because He was present. And then, He called Lazarus out of What if God’s delay in your life is making room for something greater? Tisha Alyssa Sanchez

## 2026-03-23 — Dangerous Pride

### Reflection

“Neither do I condemn you. Go, [and] from now on do not sin any more.” – (or ) There’s someone in today’s Gospel I relate to—and it’s not who you might think. I started reading the Bible every day at 13. My life revolved around church and ministry, something not many kids my age can relate to. Because of that, I often felt different—even set apart. Over time, without realizing it, I fell into the trap of thinking I was holier than others. I believed my faith somehow made me better than all my friends. Looking back, I see how dangerous that pride was. I feel real sorrow for the times I let it grow in my heart. That’s why I see myself, not in the woman accused, but in the scribes and Pharisees who stood ready to condemn. Jesus reminds us that none of us are without sin. Who are we to throw stones when we, too, are broken? The only way to truly receive God’s grace is to recognize that we are all like that woman on the ground. Yet at the same time, we are also the child to whom God says, “Neither do I condemn you.” Helene Gogna

## 2026-03-24 — Punishment And Grace

Accordingly Moses made a bronze serpent and mounted it on a pole, and whenever the serpent bit someone, the person looked at the bronze serpent and recovered.” – Our reading for today shows a deep truth about people and how God deals with them. The Israelites are discouraged, tired, and angry. They started to complain about God and Moses, just like how we tend to grumble when things are hard. We forget how faithful God has been in the past. They saw the snakes as the result of their sin and a physical sign of their dissatisfaction. But God didn’t leave them. The metal snake on a pole was His way of saving people. This is both punishment and grace. Looking at the snake, which represents their pain, also becomes their way to get better. Theologically, this is a picture of Christ’s death on the cross, where He takes our sins upon Himself and becomes the way for us to be saved. This verse tells us that our trials, even if they hurt, can bring us back to God. A lot of times, our complaints show that we don’t trust, but God’s kindness gives us a way to look to Him and be healed, even when we are in pain. Randy Borromeo 

### Prayer

God’s grace always finds a way to save. God’s mercy always makes the first move. Dear God, help me to look to You when I am in doubt, in pain, and struggling. And as I fix my gaze on You, may I be encouraged, healed, and set free. Amen.

## 2026-03-26 — The Promise

I choose patience over frustration when I see the dishes piled up or the house in disarray. I give up an extra hour of rest to prepare healthy meals for my family—because love often looks like quiet sacrifices. To follow Jesus, we must “die to ourselves.” What does this mean? It means letting go of the things that keep us from Him—our sins, our pride, our anger, our selfishness. These things separate us from God and weigh down our hearts. We know the struggles of daily life: balancing responsibilities, facing frustrations, and battling our own weaknesses. But when we surrender these things to God, it will feel lighter. I always trust that God answers my prayers and all things work for good. This allowed me to encourage others and pray for them with the belief that God’s plan will prevail. So when my sister was diagnosed with cancer and had to undergo surgery, I trusted it would all work out as it always had. A healing priest even prayed over her before I took her to the OR. ## 2026-03-29 — Who Do You Choose?

Pilate had the choice to follow his wife when she warned him about her dream. But he listened to the crowd—the same ones who greeted Jesus with waving palm branches, but later rejected Him. Sadly, he chose his career over his conscience. It’s a similar battle we experience every day when we choose to do what’s right or not. While Pontius Pilate can no longer rewrite his story, we can. Today, let’s recommit to welcome Jesus in our hearts, wave our palms, and reject the call of sin. And Lazarus stayed with the One who resurrected him. So they hosted the dinner to honor Jesus. There were also onlookers who got curious and thrilled about what Jesus did. Later, these onlookers would choose Barabbas and demand Jesus’ crucifixion. This made me ponder. “Am I like Mary, Martha, and Lazarus who glorify and thank God after He worked miracles in my life? Or am I like the crowd who forgot what He did and choose other things or people over Him? Do I thank Him through my service, presence, or offering? ## 2026-04-01 — Speak Good

He said, “Bo, you have a gift to preach! I don’t have that gift. I wish I had it. If I were you, I’d say yes to every invitation. You need to use your gift, or God might take it away from you!” There was anger in his voice. But in the process, words of honor slipped out of his lips. In a tangential way, he praised me. And my spirit rejoiced. Those words meant so much to me because I still remember them today, long after he’s gone. I believe there is a deep need to be honored by one’s parents in every human heart. We want to hear the words, “I’m proud and happy that you’re my child.” Don’t say, “Oh, Bo, I’m too old to learn.” Use your words to bless others. When you honor a person with your words, you’re not just making that person feel good—because words have power. God has imbued our words with immense power—they can create life, heal wounds, and refresh the spirit. Bo Sanchez

## 2026-04-03 — The Price Of Love

But today, I appreciate Good Friday on a much deeper level. I now understand that love is more than just words, actions, or gifts. It requires dying to self. I, too, am invited to do the same—to die to myself over and over again for the people I love. Jan Silan

And as he said this, he showed them his hands and his feet. What helps me convince clients to buy is when I present proof, particularly when I show how many major companies use and benefit from my system. They realize that if top companies use it, then they, too, can afford and benefit from it. When Jesus resurrected and journeyed with the disciples to Emmaus, they didn’t think it was Him. ## 2026-04-10 — Real Presence

### Reflection

Jesus said to them, “Come, have breakfast.” And none of the disciples dared to ask him, “Who are you?” because they realized 25-27 it was the Lord. – I have been a daily Mass goer ever since my Life in the Spirit Seminar in 1991. I can’t imagine not attending Mass and receiving communion. I struggled during the pandemic lockdowns. Yes, there were online Masses but I felt they weren’t enough. Then a friend invited me to a convent where live Masses were celebrated daily. I was allowed to join the nuns because they knew I was with The Feast. Of course, we observed proper protocols too. At a time when the world seemed bleak and hopeless, the Lord made me feel and see Him in my life—with His Real Presence in the Eucharist no less! How many times have we heard the line, “To see is to believe”? This is where faith comes into the picture: when we believe the things we do not yet see. But when we believe, just like the disciples in today’s reading, we will also see the wonders the Lord has in store for us. Adrian Panganiban 

### Prayer

Jesus, continue to reveal Yourself to me at Mass, with every communion I receive. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Fulbert of Chartres

## 2026-04-11 — Can’T Help It

### Reflection

But Peter and John replied, “You can judge for yourselves—is it better to listen to you or to God? It’s impossible for us to stop speaking 19-21 about all the things we’ve seen and heard!” – I love trying out new cafes. If I had a great experience, I can’t help but share it with others. I feel it’s the same with my experience of God but a million times greater. What He allowed me to see is something I can never just keep to myself. There are far too many miracles. Let me share the most recent one. Last December, I felt God leading me to fast for a loved one. So I gave up sweets for a month. But one day, I bargained with God for an exemption. Because I ate out with my sister and had a course meal with desserts. But it turned out that the desserts were bad. Another time, I was tempted to eat the French toast in the buffet, but my mom discouraged me, saying it was hard. God was helping me to finish my fast and gave me the graces I needed. On the last day of my fast, God moved so mightily in the person I was praying for. Something unprecedented happened. And now this person is on his road to recovery and healing. Marianne Mencias 

### Prayer

Jesus, give me all the grace I need to do Your most holy will. Saint Stanislaus, bishop and martyr,

## 2026-04-12 — Final Grace

### Reflection

In his great mercy he has given us new birth into a living hope through the resurrection of Jesus Christ from the dead. 22-24 – 1 They say that hell is for those who Second Sunday of Easter refuse to believe. We see these two choices play out in the two thieves with Jesus on Calvary. One mocked Jesus, while the good thief received heaven when he humbly chose to trust in Him. At the end of our lives, the final judgment will be the fulfillment of God’s mercy. Because the Father can’t look at us except through the wounds of Christ, through which we have been redeemed. It is also by God’s mercy that souls have the freedom to choose, even if that choice leads to eternal separation from Him, a.k.a. hell. In today’s Gospel, we find Jesus lovingly admonishing Thomas to “stop doubting and believe.” Friend, believe today that God’s mercy is so much greater than your sins. Trust in His great love for you! As Fr. Steve Tynan said, “Nothing we do now will make us deserve the gift of eternal life. It is God’s free gift to us.” Marjorie Duterte

Lord, I believe. Help my unbelief. Jesus, King of Mercy, I trust in You! St. Zeno of Verona,

## 2026-04-13 — He Will Enable You

### Reflection

“Lord . . . enable your servants to speak your word with all boldness, as you stretch forth [your] hand to heal, and signs and wonders are done through the name of your holy servant Jesus.” – Being a missionary family in Timor Leste was challenging at times. We received rejection and even threats from people who did not understand why we were there. They were not open to what we wanted to share with them because they were set in their own beliefs, culture, and ways. Despite the difficulties and our fears and weaknesses, we continued serving the Lord and His people. It was God who called us to the mission, so He would equip us. We surrendered all our plans and asked Him to embolden and use us. We did our part and learned the local language, translated our teachings and songs, and let Him move. After some months— by His mercy and grace—we were able to witness His “signs and wonders” in our mission area. In the same way, when you choose to serve God in your everyday life, He will enable you. Just surrender everything to Him. Tina Santiago Rodriguez

Lord, following and serving You can be difficult. Help me to be faithful to You and Your mission, especially in my daily life. Saint Martin I, pope and martyr,

## 2026-04-14 — Happy, Peaceful,

### Reflection

“You should not be surprised at my saying, ‘You must be born again.’” – NIV When I was still in the corporate world, I met a guy who was at the peak of his career—successful, making a ton of money, and living the kind of life most people would envy. On the surface, he seemed to have it all. But deep down, something was off. He felt empty, like he was just going through the motions, drifting through life without real Then everything came crashing down. He lost his job and a lot of his friends. Even his family, whom he had been supporting, turned their backs on him. At rock bottom, he I’ll never forget one conversation we had. He said, “I may have lost everything I had, but today I’ve found everything I need because I accepted Jesus into my life.” Now, he’s living a much simple life in the province with his wife and kids—a far cry from the life he used to have. But he always says, “I’ve never been happier. I’ve never had more peace. I’ve never felt more alive until I was born again.” Joel N. Saludares

Jesus, may Your love and grace transform and renew me so I can follow Your ways every day. Amen.

## 2026-04-15 — Priceless

### Reflection

“For God so loved the world that He gave His only Son, so that everyone who believes in him might not perish but might have eternal 6-7, 8-9 life.” – I’m an audiologist in a 47-year- old hearing aid business. Premium hearing aids have always been high- priced. Why? Because you’ve compressed such a powerful computer—capable of performing a million calculations per second—into a tiny device that fits in your ear. And here I am, before the Blessed Sacrament. I realized that all of God’s love which moved Him to send His only Son to die in our place is contained in a tiny piece of wafer! If that wasn’t enough, His omnipotence, power, and majesty that the universe can’t contain is in that tiny host too. How sad that only 69 percent of Mass goers believe in the real presence of Jesus in the Eucharist. Ironically, demons recognize the power contained in the consecrated host. They’re tortured by its presence. If Catholics realize how precious the host is, we’d drop to our knees in reverence! May we recognize the real presence of Jesus in every host we receive during Holy Communion. Ronna Singson Ledesma

Saint Hunna

## 2026-04-16 — Who Do You Follow?

### Reflection

“We must obey God rather than human beings!” – “My body, my rules! How dare you deny me of abortion? You don’t know my story!” “Cheating is a lifestyle. Do not expect everyone to have a good marriage.” I’ve read and heard these woke arguments too many times. Sadly, this logic implies that if you advocate for the truth based on Scriptures and Church teachings, then you are unloving. Exclusive. Out of touch. In today’s world when most people care only about “their truth,” it takes greater courage to speak about and obey God’s Truth. It’s easier to just go with the majority. But whenever I hesitate to follow His will because of what other people might think, I catch myself reflecting, “Who is my moral compass? Who do I desire to please? Myself, the world, or my Lord?” It’s challenging to obey God’s commandments. But it’s not impossible. After all, we have the Holy Spirit “whom God has given to those who obey him” (). I can only surrender to His grace to help me live a life that pleases Him. Maymay Salvosa Trinidad 

### Prayer

Father, grant me Your Spirit of wisdom, courage, humility, and truth, that I may follow You no matter how difficult it is. With You, all things are possible. Amen.

## 2026-04-17 — Be The Miracle

### Reflection

Then Jesus took the loaves, gave thanks, and distributed them to those who were reclining, and also as much of the fish as they wanted. In Bruce Almighty, a 2003 comedy movie, God (played by Morgan Freeman) challenged Bruce (Jim Carrey), “You want a miracle? Be the miracle!” The Gospel today exhorts us to be the miracles too. How? Focus on Jesus, not on the crowd. The “crowd” represents things that intimidate me into anxiety. But when I refocus on Jesus, He transforms these into faith and trust. Give your all, no matter how small. I see myself as that little boy with five loaves and two fish of limitations. In surrendering my all to Jesus, He turns my smallness into miracles. The loaves and fish in a little boy’s hands remain few until they’re entrusted to the mighty hands of Jesus. I’ve witnessed many miracles multiply when fully surrendered to God’s hands. Continue the miracle every step of the way. The Twelve Apostles started with little and ended up with 12 wicker baskets of surplus. Jesus was teaching them: “The miracle doesn’t end here. You have the power to continue the miracle! Obet Cabrillas 

### Prayer

Jesus, allow me to focus only in You—to give my all, to surrender all to Your hands, and continue the miracle. In Jesus’ name. Amen.

Saint Donan

## 2026-04-18 — Do Something About It

### Reflection

Brothers, select from among you seven reputable men, filled with the Spirit and wisdom, whom we shall appoint to this task. – I talked to a young guy who expressed his woes about the way things were done in an organization he volunteered for. There were certain missteps he noticed among the members and problems he predicted that came to pass. He said, “Why does it seem like I’m the only one noticing these things? Doesn’t anyone else care?” His words struck me. At that moment, it led me to a question I felt I had to ask: “Is it that they don’t care, or is it that you were just the first to notice these concerns? Because there’s a big difference between not caring and not being aware.” God gave us certain gifts and perspectives that only we have. It’s no accident that there are points of view that only we possess, and actions that we are in the right position to do. With prayer and discernment, let God reveal to us how we can work with Him—through our words and deeds—toward the good of those He has sent our way. Mon Reyes 

### Prayer

Jesus, give me the confidence to speak and act, the discernment to know when and how to do such, and the humility to do them with the right posture of my heart. Amen.

St. Athanasia of Aegina

## 2026-04-19 — Emmaus Walk

### Reflection

Now that very day two of them were going to a village seven miles from Jerusalem called Emmaus. – Jesus sets His face toward Jerusalem. The Cross, resurrection, Third Sunday of Easter ascension, and Pentecost all happened on the big stage: Jerusalem. So these two disciples are walking the wrong way: away from Jerusalem! One was named Cleopas. The other was unnamed. Because he is you and me. We claim we follow Jesus, but still we keep getting it wrong. The good news is that even though we are walking the wrong way, Jesus comes and walks with us. We don’t have to perform adequately and get it perfectly to get God to walk with us. Jesus simply comes. We don’t recognize Him at first. Pope Francis aptly put it in one word: accompaniment. Jesus started with a question, not with a sermon. Why? Theologian Paul Tillich said, “Most sermons are answers to questions that nobody’s asking.” So Jesus asked, “What are you discussing as you walk along?” Even though they are walking the wrong way, He sought to understand their hearts and minds. Just like Jesus, let’s walk with others who’ve lost their bearings. Be near. Walk. Ask. Listen. Converse. Repeat. Until they invite us to stay and find Jesus. Jon Escoto 

### Prayer

Jesus, walk with me again as I walk to help others. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Alphege of Canterbury

## 2026-04-20 — Courage And Wisdom

But the Spirit gave Stephen such wisdom that when he spoke, they could not refute him. 29-30 – GNT Have you ever been in a situation where you had to defend your position or choice before a group of people or even just one person? How did you feel? How did it turn out? Many of us will face moments like these throughout our lives. Even among close friends and family, disagreements are inevitable because we In today’s world, faithfully following God often means encountering people who don’t share our beliefs or values. But as Christians, we are called to live a countercultural life. We are not meant to conform to the ways of the world. Instead, we are called to be salt and light, standing firm in our faith So the next time you find yourself in such a situation, take courage like Saint Stephen. Do not be afraid. Turn to God who is the source of all knowledge, wisdom, guidance, and counsel. Let the Holy Spirit strengthen you with the grace and courage you need to defend your faith and stand by your godly choices. Teresa G. Dumadag

St. Agnes of Montepulciano

“I am the bread of life. Whoever comes to me –8:1 will never go hungry, and whoever believes in me will never be thirsty.” – Ever since the Internet came, I’ve always loved sharing anything under the sun on my social media platforms. I posted anything I liked that I thought others would like too. I created posts of good food, beautiful places, and good deals. Since 2023, I’ve been doing content creation focused mostly on my travels. Because of my consistency in creating videos, I was awarded as a Rising Star Traveller and Catholic Social Media Female Influencer of 2024. Brands paid me and people began recognizing me in public places. The awards, success, money, and fame seem fulfilling, but those didn’t It’s easy to be blinded with worldly offers, but at the end of the day, it’s temporary. May this remind us to prioritize our spiritual growth and nourishment, not forgetting to thank and glorify the Lord. Let us seek the Bread of Life more because only He can truly satisfy. Maine Ybiernas

## 2026-04-22 — Always And Wherever

Those who had been scattered preached the word wherever they went. – NIV A lawyer defending the rights of children. A teacher enlightening the minds of the future. A businessman proudly supporting his employees through humanitarian practices. A doctor administering God’s healing touch through medicine. One of them still serves in the music ministry. Another still leads worship. Yet another runs a small prayer group and Bible study in a country where religion isn’t openly talked about. And most of them, like me, ended up as parents—leading little “churches,” These were my youth leaders, the ones I looked up to as a teenager. They inspired me to offer my best years in service to God’s Kingdom. They had so much zeal and passion that I wondered why some of them stopped being part of a community years after graduating from the youth ministry. ## 2026-04-23 — My Brother’S Keeper

### Reflection

“Do you understand what you are reading?” – In January 2025, Manila became a sea of garbage following the Traslacion attended by an estimated eight million devotees of the Black Nazarene. In contrast, the Iglesia ni Cristo rally held a week later, which was attended by 1.2 million members, cleaned up after themselves and left the area spotless. I can’t help but feel saddened that some religions rooted in the Bible miss out on the fulfillment of God’s promises because they don’t believe Jesus is the Messiah. Yet, I also marvel at their deep devotion to God’s commands as written in Scripture. Their commitment is heartfelt and sincere, clearly reflected in the way they live each day. As Catholics, we share the Good News because we know we are accountable for all others’ salvation. I hope to see the day when we can also be accountable to each other in attitude, speech, thoughts, and actions. After all, we are our brother’s keeper and should be responsible for our good and everyone else’s. Cecil Lim

## 2026-04-24 — Ananias Or Paul?

### Reflection

“Saul, my brother, the Lord has sent me, Jesus who appeared to you on the way by which you came, that you may regain your sight and be filled with the holy Spirit.” – I was 14 when we first started attending The Feast. As any parent would with a teenager in tow, my mom persuaded me to attend Youth Home (called Youth High then), much to my absolute dismay. I was a shy kid who was never good at making friends but we had a deal: Try three sessions, and if I don’t like it, I can stop going. Years later, I’m When Jesus sent Ananias to heal Paul, I was reminded of the community I grew up in and how I wouldn’t have the relationship I have with Jesus today without them. Their openness, guidance, and love toward the Father opened my eyes to what serving and worshiping the Lord truly means. We are not meant to journey alone. Like Paul, God will send the most unexpected person to walk with you. Or like Ananias, He will call you to serve. I pray we welcome that call. Gemma Casimsiman

## 2026-04-25 — Let Go

### Reflection

Cast all your worries upon him because 1 he cares for you. – 1 As my adult children venture into the world on their own, I’m learning Evangelist to step back. This new dynamic in our relationship has brought me to some tough emotional places. It’s hard to stand aside when my sons make life choices that are different from mine. I can’t help but worry when they head down unexpected paths, fearful about what the future may hold for them. Watching them navigate broken relationships, relentless work pressures, and disturbing health issues can be disheartening. When the weight of letting go feels overwhelming, the verse above serves as my anchor, giving me comfort amidst my worries and reminding me that I don’t carry the burden alone. God works in the sidelines in ways I can’t see. He is faithful, even when His answers don’t align with my desires. Although my sons’ journeys may not always go as I envision, I trust that God is guiding them. In my waiting, I learn to surrender my fears, resting in the quiet assurance that God’s perfect plan for them will manifest in ways far greater than I could imagine. Marie G. Ferrer

## 2026-04-26 — Do You Bark Or Heel?

### Reflection

“The sheep follow him, because they recognize his voice.” – Our dog is one smart canine. He can tell if a person outside our gate is Fourth Sunday of Easter a stranger or one of us. If a member of the family or household rings the doorbell, he doesn’t bark from inside the house. But if it’s a mailman or a Shopee deliveryman, he yaps. Does he smell us from afar? Can he sense our presence even from yards away through walls and closed doors? One thing’s for sure. When I arrive home and open the front door, he’s there waiting to greet me. I joke that our dog is better than my kids or my husband because they don’t even come to greet me when I get home. No wonder people call dogs man’s best friend! Reflecting on our Gospel, I realize I should be more like my doggie when it comes to the Lord. Do I recognize His presence from afar? Do I bark and fight back when the enemy of my soul approaches? Do I wait upon God—ever eager to greet and recognize Him when He manifests Himself? By God’s grace, I want to be His best friend. Rissa Singson Kawpeng 

### Prayer

Lord Jesus, I want to know Your voice and recognize Your guiding hand at every turn in my life. Amen.

Saint Riquier

## 2026-04-27 — Go To Your Maker

### Reflection

“I am the good shepherd, and I know mine and mine know me.” – ; 43:3-4 There were two wrecked Honda cars in the parking lot when I brought my car to the shop. The smashed cars were parked beside each other and reminded me of the phrase, “Misery loves company.” They looked like two unhappy, damaged people who met up to share their woes. Nothing wrong with that. People tend to relate better with others who experienced similar brokenness. These two cars could be repaired in any other shop that’s lower than the Honda dealer. But their owners opted to bring them here because they knew that only the maker of their car could give the best solution. That applies to us too. The best way for us to be fixed from our brokenness is to go back to our Maker, because no one knows us better than Him. Jesus is the Good Shepherd who knows us. He will take care of us and restore us back to our form, so we can do what we were designed to do. Alvin Fabella

God and allow Him to restore you. Lord, I trust that You who called me to Your eternal glory through Christ will restore, confirm, strengthen, and establish me after I have suffered a little (cf. 1 Peter 5:10). Saint Zita,

## 2026-04-28 — Stubborn Heart

### Reflection

“My sheep listen to my voice; I know them, and they follow me.” – NLT Do you have a hard time listening to a loved one? A parent, sibling, wife, relative, or a close friend? Oh, yes. In my case, it’s my wife. One Sunday afternoon, we arrived at The Feast Novotel and my wife said we should take the elevator instead of the escalator. “It’s nearer our venue and we’re carrying the big crucifix and Mama Mary,” she reasoned. I irritably replied, “We’ll be in the other room today so this is nearer.” I didn’t read the messages in our group chat and my wife was right. When she saw my face after realizing that I made a mistake, she stared at me with a look that said, “I told you so.” I saw her Today, Jesus invites us to listen to Him. Accept who He is in our lives. Do not let our own thoughts, perceptions, and pride stop us from seeing the God who openly offers His life and presence in our lives. Mike Ybiernas

## 2026-04-29 — Merciful Judge

### Reflection

“If anyone hears my words but does –13:5 not keep them, I do not judge that person. For I did not come to judge the world, but to save the world.” – When I was younger, I imagined God as the “Big Guy Upstairs” watching everyone like a policeman judging our every move in His gigantic CCTV. So I avoided sin out of fear of being caught. I thought that God anticipated my every mistake so He could punish me. So I became hard on myself, and in turn, hard on others as well. Once, I went to confession and ran down every little sin from my long list. The priest stopped me in the middle and said, “You know what? You have a scrupulous conscience. Why are you confessing those things? Those are not even considered sins.” I replied, “I guess I’m so afraid to not live up to God’s standards.” He said, “Standards are good. When everything is said and done, it’s true that God will judge us. But I want you to remember that our judge is a merciful Judge.” While it’s true that God sees our sins and mistakes, know that He also sees your kind deeds and loving acts. Are you too hard on yourself? Remember, Jesus, our Judge, died for us because He longs to save us, not punish us. Velden Lim 

### Prayer

Jesus, give me Your eyes of mercy so I can love myself and others the way You love me. St. Catherine of Siena, virgin and Doctor of the Church,

The God of this people Israel chose our ancestors and exalted the people during their sojourn in the land of Egypt. With uplifted arm 25, 27 he led them out of it. – Being a provider and breadwinner is both a privilege and a struggle. As a husband and father of two, I often carry the weight of ensuring my family’s future, especially as we work toward financial stability. Like Israel in the wilderness, there are moments of uncertainty. But Paul’s words remind me: God is in control. He has been faithful before. He will be faithful again. Paul’s message in reminds me of how God has always been faithful in His plans—whether for Israel or for my own life. As a Feast builder, I see how God has been leading our community, just as He led His people through history. In His perfect time, He will provide—not just for our needs but for the mission He has placed in our hearts. Stephen Nellas

## 2026-05-01 — When Crisis Comes

### Reflection

“Do not let your hearts be troubled.” One day, a woman approached (or )me and asked, “Brother Bo, you look so calm. Do you ever get worried?” I laughed and said, “Of course! I’m a I’m also an entrepreneur. Ask any entrepreneur—we eat problems for breakfast, lunch, and dinner, including snacks. It’s stressful, and yes, I do worry. I’m also involved in our mercy ministries, and there are problems and conflicts there too. But here’s the thing. I do two powerful things when it comes to worry. So, if you are stressed or going through a crisis right now, let me share them with you. First, I focus on what I can control. I plan and come up with action steps to resolve them. And then I feel so much better. But for the things I cannot control, the magic sauce is I surrender them to God. I have zero control anyway, so why not give it to the Lord who knows all things? Friend, when you have done your part, believe that God will do the rest. Then you will have peace that only He can give. Bo Sanchez

Father, give me wisdom to know what to do in the areas where I have control, and the trust to surrender those matters that I have no control of. Amen.

## 2026-05-02 — Jesus Is God

### Reflection

“If you know me, then you will also know my Father.” – The Gospel today reminds me of a truth I learned from my theology professors—that Jesus is the definitive revelation of who God is. In other words, if you want to know the character of God, look to the character of Christ. See the entirety of His life, death, and resurrection. Know His Words and way of life, and from there, you will know who God is, what He is like, and the life He invites you to live. Jesus is fully man and fully divine. He is full of grace and full of truth. He is gentle and powerful. He is servant and Lord. He is both merciful and just. And that’s just to name a few. So, whenever life hits hard and your faith in God is shaken and tested, look to Jesus and the Cross. When problems pile up and you don’t know what to do, look to Jesus and the Cross. You will see your God, who is always loving, selfless, and faithful. May the knowledge of this truth strengthen your faith and lead you to live your life loving, selfless, and faithful. Mike Viñas 

### Prayer

Father, help me know You more as I know and follow Jesus. Mold me to be more like Jesus in everything, every day. Saint Athanasius, bishop and Doctor of the Church,

## 2026-05-03 — The Ultimate Superhost

### Reflection

“In My Father’s house are many dwelling places . . . for I go to prepare a place for you . . . I will come again and receive you to 1 Myself, that where I am, there you may be also.” – Fifth Sunday of Easter Today marks my 36th year on Earth, and tomorrow it will be my mother’s 56th. Since our birthdays are close to each other, we usually make it a double celebration by booking a nice Airbnb somewhere for a short family staycation. That’s why our reading today about Jesus preparing a place for us made me smile. It’s like picturing the most amazing Airbnb ever, with Jesus as the ultimate Superhost! I’d spend a lot of time planning and scrolling to find the perfect spot for our family. But this time, Jesus has already chosen for me and prepared everything. And instead of getting a digital door code, He will personally welcome me and dwell in that place with me! What a beautiful glimpse of all the endless birthdays I’ll celebrate in heaven when the time comes—an eternity of joy with Him in a place of unimaginable wonder! Gretchen Veran

Jesus already prepared a place for you when He calls you home. How does this truth change the way you live your life? Lord Jesus, may I live each day in perfect peace knowing You have a room for me in our Father’s house. Saints Philip and James, Apostles,

## 2026-05-04 — Beyond Our Control

### Reflection

“The Advocate, the holy Spirit that the Father will send in my name—he will teach you everything and remind you of all that I told you.” – Last year, my grandfather was diagnosed with aspiration pneumonia, and he was hospitalized Those weeks were tough. Lolo was 93 years old and hard of hearing, but he could still understand what was happening. He is a doctor himself, but he was helpless about his condition. This made him very uneasy and impatient. We kept him busy with the newspaper, TV, and video calls. We explained his diagnosis repeatedly when he would forget. We were at his bedside 24/7. But it became discouraging when he himself asked, “Why am I still here? What is there left to do?” We knew that this was beyond our control. We asked him to pray for us when he was in pain. We wrote the intentions he could pray for and posted them on the walls. This calmed him down, and we knew that the Holy Spirit had taken over. Fr. Migs Ramirez 

### Prayer

Father, may I do my best to follow Your will today. But when weakness and hopelessness set in, take over with Your Holy Spirit. Amen.

Saint Venerius

## 2026-05-05 — The Purpose Of Trials

### Reflection

It is through many tribulations that we must enter the Kingdom of God. – This week has been incredibly challenging. One of my best friends— someone who is exceptionally health- conscious and athletic—was recently diagnosed with leukemia. Our home Feast is facing difficulties due to a city government order temporarily closing our venue over permit issues. On top of that, I’ve been experiencing frequent pain in my lower right abdomen. Tribulations are exhausting. Oh, what I would give for a safe, comfortable life without problems! But today’s reading reminds us that trials are part of our journey to God’s Kingdom. If tribulations are the path that leads us there, then perhaps they are not just obstacles but opportunities for Knowing this doesn’t make challenges disappear, but it does give them purpose. The promise of entering God’s Kingdom brings hope and peace, making every struggle more bearable. Instead of resisting trials, I choose to embrace them with faith, trusting that God’s plan is greater than my understanding. Erika Mendoza Ventura 

### Prayer

Lord, I surrender in full faith. I will trust that You are in control. St. Hilary of Arles,

I think, “I’ve got this!”—until my body (or my wife) reminds Jesus said, “I have told you this so that my joy may be in you and your joy may be complete” (). His love isn’t about just getting by—it’s about fullness, peace, and joy. How do I remain in His love? 1. Pause before burnout. I stop, breathe, and pray. I am not God. ## 2026-05-10 — Obedience Out Of Love

### Reflection

“If you love me, you will keep my commandments.” – When I was young, I was so scared to do anything that would Sixth Sunday of Easter offend God. I pictured the Father as someone who was so stern that if I sinned, He would give me a spanking. This fear, rather than love, fueled my attempts to be good. I walked a tightrope of obedience, scared of the consequences of falling short. But time, and a deepening relationship with God, reshaped my understanding. My image of a stern disciplinarian began to soften, revealing the loving Father beneath. I now perceived His commandments as the gentle guidance of a parent who only desires the best for his child. It dawned on me that He never challenges us with a dismissive, “Go your own way and see what happens.” Instead, He offers a path of love and invites us to walk it with Him. Obedience isn’t about avoiding punishment—it’s about responding to God’s love with our love. Thus, our obedience becomes an expression of love rather than fear. Reng D. Morelos

## 2026-05-11 — As Within, So Without

### Reflection

When she and the members of her household were baptized . . . she said, “Come and stay at In close to two decades of coaching, I’ve witnessed this truth: Internal transformation overflows externally. I once coached a COO of a global company. She was burnt out, overworked, and often sick, yet still pushed herself to keep performing. She kept saying yes to clients, micromanaged her team, and came home too exhausted to truly be with her children. Through coaching, she discovered that her drive came from a childhood longing for affirmation. When she healed that wounded part of her heart, everything changed. Her team felt more trusted. Her clients were seamlessly handed over to her employees. Her children experienced a more present, joyful mom. As within, so without. Today’s readings show the same principle. The Lord opened Lydia’s heart and her first instinct was to act: She welcomed Paul and his companions into her home. And in the Gospel, Jesus reminded the disciples that with the Spirit’s help, their testimony must flow even amid persecution. When we are healed within, love overflows. Edwin S. Soriano

## 2026-05-12 — Dream Home

“But now I am going to the one who sent me, and not one of you asks me, ‘Where are you going?’ But because I told you this, grief has filled your hearts.” – Building our dream house was an exciting journey—one we prayed and worked hard for. But along the way, financial setbacks struck and we had to stop construction. We were like the disciples in our reading who were sorrowful when Jesus said He was leaving. Doubt crept in. Was this dream really meant for us? We surrendered to God, trusting that if this was His plan, He would make a way. And He did. After nine months of waiting, praying, and doing what we could, construction resumed. Resources came, and slowly, our dream home became a reality. Today, as we live in it, we see how the delay strengthened our faith. The pause wasn’t a setback but a lesson—teaching us to rely not on our own understanding but on His divine provision. God’s timing is always perfect. Surrendering to His plan brings greater blessings than we can ever imagine. Maine Ybiernas

## 2026-05-13 — Groping For God

### Reflection

So that people might seek God, –18:1 even perhaps grope for him and find him, though indeed he is not far from any one 13, 14 of us. – Sometimes, I feel like I’m playing hide-and-seek with God, but I’m the one blindfolded. For instance, when I’m engrossed in office work or trying to understand why I’m bald, I get so caught up in the details that I forget to look up. This reminds me of an old cashew tree in our front yard. As a child, I believed that the best cashews were always the ones high up. I’d climb and strain, getting scratched up, only to discover that they weren’t even ripe. However, the sweetest cashews, bursting with flavor, were always the ones that had fallen right at my feet. That’s me “groping” for God. It’s not about some grand, epic quest to distant places. Instead, it’s about noticing the simple things around me—a kind word from someone, a shared laugh with my wife, or the sunlight reflecting on my forehead. He’s not hiding in some far-off galaxy. He’s present in the ordinary, every day. I just need to stop climbing so high and look around. Perhaps even remove the blindfold. And maybe, I’ll realize that He was there all along, closer than I could have imagined. Rene Espinosa 

### Prayer

God is present in the people around you and in the simple things you see. Lord, help me see You when You show Yourself all around me. Our Lady of Fatima,

### Saint of the Day

Our Lady of Fatima

## 2026-05-14 — Build Intentional

Instead, I have called you friends, for everything that I learned from my Father I have made known to you. – Do you have people in your life who know who you truly are? I’m referring to friends who don’t just know your hobbies, favorite color, or crushes, but also your deepest desires, biggest battles, greatest fears, weirdest quirks, or absurd obsessions. Judas was part of the original Twelve Apostles—a very tight inner circle. But the sad part is that no one really knew who he was nor the poor state of his heart. I imagine that if he truly opened himself to Jesus or another disciple, he might have had the strength to overcome his greatest temptation. Let yourself be known and seen. ## 2026-05-15 — Grief Is Joy’S

### Reflection

“You will grieve, but your grief will suddenly turn to wonderful joy.” – There are three levels of happiness: Fun. Happiness. Joy. Fun is dependent on the moment. It’s what you experience when you feel the sudden rush of dopamine, like riding a roller coaster or taking a bite of your favorite dessert. Happiness is a little deeper. It is dependent on the situation. You feel it when you get a pay raise, enjoy a beach vacation, or achieve a goal. But joy is the deepest level. Joy depends not on a moment or a situation but on your disposition. This joy is what keeps us going and allows us to smile in the midst of life’s tragedies and trials. In the animated movie Inside Out 2, Joy said to Sadness, “Wherever you go, I go.” We often think that sadness is the opposite of joy. The truth is, they are twins. We are able to appreciate and savor the sweetness of joy if we have tasted the bitterness of grief. Are you grieving? This pain will soon pass. Joy is just around the corner. You may not understand it now, but the joy that’s coming will pale in comparison to the pain you’re experiencing. Velden Lim 

### Prayer

Lord, give me Your hope and joy that no tragedy and trial can ever take away. Amen.

## 2026-05-16 — En-Courage

“The brothers encouraged him . . . ” There I was, hanging on to dear life as I climbed a tall tree. I desperately pulled myself toward the platform set atop some branches. But as I held the ropes and took the steps, I slowly but surely lost energy. And when I looked down, I realized I was high above the ground! Aside from fear, failure came to mind. I was inches away from the platform but I couldn’t go further. Then I heard my teammates shouting at the top of their lungs, “You can do it!” Their words empowered me and I said, “I can do this!” Like magic, I found a second wind of strength that propelled me up to the designated platform. Their encouragement gave me the energy and courage I needed to go higher and get to Friend, maybe it’s time you surround yourself with encouraging people. Better still, be the encouragement people need wherever God has planted you. We all need each other to be better. J Yogawin

## 2026-05-17 — Discipline

But I also believe the world needs disciplined men. While I encourage his exploration, there are moments when I must step in, guide, and discipline him. One day, I caught him trying to plug the electric fan into the socket. I immediately stopped him, scolded him, and reminded him that only Daddy and Mommy should handle it for now. Setting rules isn’t about limiting him but protecting him from harm. I see discipline as an expression of love— being present, guiding, and ensuring his well-being. I believe God parents us the same way. He disciplines us not to restrict our freedom but to protect us from making harmful choices. He cares deeply for us, setting boundaries for our good. ## 2026-05-18 — Plain And Simple

### Reflection

His disciples said, “Now you are talking plainly, and not in any figure of speech.” – I had the rare opportunity to teach homiletics to a class of seminarians and priests. I told them that if they’d have a takeaway from my lecture, I hope it would be to use stories in their homilies. The reason I fell in love with the Lord and His Word was because I attended a prayer meeting where Scripture was explained in a way that I—then a 14-year-old who only wanted to go to parties and get to know boys—understood. Scripture stopped being a textbook I studied in my Religion subject and became a life manual that spoke God’s personal message to me every day. The truth is, facts don’t change people. If they did, nobody would smoke cigarettes given the gruesome warnings on its boxes. But stories smuggle in even truths too complex or painful for lay people. That’s why Jesus used parables to explain abstract concepts like God’s Kingdom and the Father’s mercy. Wanna share Jesus with others? No need to learn homiletics. Just tell them your story. Rissa Singson Kawpeng 

### Prayer

Lord, let Scripture become plain and simple to me so I can apply it in my personal life. Amen.

## 2026-05-19 — The Presider

“I pray for them.” – A cartoonist depicted a church scene with the priest falling asleep with his own sermon, while the pews were filled with blocks of ice (symbolizing how the church people have grown cold in their worship and love of God). His solution was to bring wrestling superstars to march with the priest during the entrance procession, complete with World Wrestling Entertainment fireworks, while the church choir sings to heavy metal music. Oh, if only we know the truth. Do you know that during the Mass, Jesus is the real Presider? That’s why strict liturgists would never call the priest “the presider.” Through Jesus, and by the power of His Holy Spirit, God delivers the grace He promised. Again, liturgists don’t call the priest as “the celebrant.” Because we, God’s children participating in the Mass, are also co-celebrants, celebrating these gifts. Genuine worship is our response to this great mystery. No lively sermons, no good sound system, no beautiful music? No problem! Jesus prays for us in the liturgy. Grace is undiminished for us and our family, the Body of Christ. Jon Escoto

“I speak this in the world so that they may share my joy completely. I gave them your word, and the world hated them.” 35-36 – My faith demands I speak out against injustice, yet fear often silences me. Social media, with its potential for backlash, can feel like a minefield. It’s easier to remain silent to avoid the discomfort of confrontation. But silence, I’ve come to realize from my Social Justice and Advocacy class, is not neutral. It’s complicity. It allows suffering to continue, betraying the very core of my Christian faith. This realization is unsettling but also a call to action. The Gospel calls me to be a voice for the voiceless, to stand up for justice, even when it’s difficult. It requires courage, humility, and a willingness to step outside my comfort zone. This journey is challenging, with setbacks and disappointments. ## 2026-05-22 — Repetitive Questions

### Reflection

Peter was distressed that he had said to him a third time, “Do you love me?” – Bert helped me discover a pet peeve I call “repetitive questioners.” He asks me a question. I answer it with information he needs. He asks me whether I am 100 percent sure. I say yes. He responds with a skeptical look. I don’t know whether it’s distrust, inattentiveness, or an intricate plan to annoy me, but this interaction has been a regular lesson on patience. Today, Jesus was the repetitive questioner. If I were in Peter’s place, my face would have shown upset, disbelief, and utter annoyance—the same reactions I had for Bert. But I realized Jesus wasn’t trying to be a pain. He could be asking me the same question not because He demanded assurance but because I needed to hear myself and firmly believe in my response. When I said I love Him, did I really mean it? Have my actions been proof of that love, or is it purely lip service? Friends, what we say, we must believe. As we preach God’s love, our actions must support and reflect that love to others. Let us answer God’s call to love and tend to His sheep. Osy Erica- Padilla

## 2026-05-23 — Focus On God

### Reflection

Jesus said to him, “What if I want him to remain until I come? What concern is it of yours? You follow me.” – “What about him/her?” Aren’t we all guilty of looking into other people’s business? Aren’t we prone to envy and comparison when we see others enjoying the things we pray for? Even worse, aren’t we inclined to notice when God deals favorably with people we think live in disobedience to Him? We often look at others and tend to forget our own blessings, our own mission and ministry, and our own sins. We watch out for other people’s punishments, as if it has become our own version of justice or vindication. But Jesus reprimanded Peter when he kept asking about the person who will betray Him. It took his mind away from the mission. Jesus reminded him to focus on Him, and no one else. It’s God alone who knows how to deal with His children, This is a reminder for us to focus on God and on the things He entrusted to us. We all have enough on our plate; let’s not add other’s concerns anymore because it takes our heart away from our true purpose. Karren Renz P. Seña

The car of my sister-in-law, who lives beside us, was carnapped right in front of our compound. Thank God, their family’s lives were spared. I thought this kind of scene happens only in the movies, but this incident traumatized me. Every time we entered our village, I looked at the side mirror and made sure that no car was following us. ## 2026-05-25 — Wealth To Love

### Reflection

Looking at the man, Jesus felt 1 (or Genesis genuine love for him. – NLT (or . 3. 5. 6-7 Jesus loves the poor. But here’s or Judith 13:18-19)what we may not understand of find (or John hard to accept: Jesus loves the rich as well. In His infinite wisdom, God the Blessed Virgin Mary, has allowed some of His children to Mother of the Church live in poverty and others to live in wealth. Take Abraham, for instance. He was prosperous with land and cattle. Solomon, known for his immense wisdom, also enjoyed great wealth. Job, a faithful servant of God, was the wealthiest man of his time. Throughout Scripture, God never condemned these men for their riches. So, why did Jesus tell the rich man to sell everything and give to the poor, but never demanded the same of Abraham, Solomon, or Job? The difference lies in this: The rich man didn’t possess money—money possessed him. Wealth itself isn’t the issue. It’s what we do with it and how we let it impact us. Being rich is not inherently wrong. What matters is the process of becoming rich and how we use our wealth. If the pursuit of wealth is moral, just, and rooted in love—then it can be a good cause, especially if it’s used to love others extravagantly. Arun Gogna

We see people chasing titles, recognition, and admiration. But Jesus reminds us that His Kingdom works differently. Everyone wants to be on top, but not everyone is willing to do the hard work. True greatness isn’t about power or prestige—it’s about service and sacrifice. As a mother, I see this every day. The world may not notice the meals cooked, the laundry folded, or the late nights spent comforting a child, but God does. These small acts of love and service, done with a willing heart, are what truly matter. Jesus Himself set the example. He washed the feet of His disciples. He humbled Himself for others. ## 2026-05-27 — Men And Women

Cagayan de Oro City was one of the worst-hit areas by the storm. But all was not lost. And as it has been said time and again, every crisis brings with it opportunity. ## 2026-05-29 — A Nudge Of Mercy

### Reflection

Offer hospitality to one another 1 without grumbling. – 1 One night, a thief broke into our house. I went downstairs for a midnight snack and noticed the back door open. Something moved. Then I saw a cap on the floor. No, it was on a man’s head! He was curled up near the front door. I shouted, “Who are you?” Panicked, he answered, “Sorry, I was just hungry.” He had come to steal food. He had no job, no money, and hadn’t eaten for days. I questioned him, towering over him, and he was scared. I could have been angry, but I felt a nudge of mercy. I told him to stay put, went to the kitchen, and packed food. I handed it to him along with some money for meals and a ride. He asked for work, but I had nothing to offer. I noticed he was barefoot, so I gave him my Before he left, I felt another nudge—to pray for him. He thanked me and walked away. I never saw him again. To this day, I wonder about him. But I hope that small act of love planted a seed of goodness in his heart, just as it planted mercy in mine. Paolo Galia

## 2026-05-30 — Love Beyond Arguments

### Reflection

“Neither will I tell you by what authority I am doing these things.” – My dad and I are “best friends.” By best friends, I mean the opposite. In my teenage years, we’d argue over the smallest things—whether I should iron my school uniform or why he wouldn’t let me bike outside. I’d get emotional and we’d argue almost every day. Now that I am older, I learned to protect my peace, regulate my emotions, and let go of futile arguments with him. He still finds faults in little things. But now, I know why. The little child in him hasn’t healed yet, especially since he came from a broken family. I now see why Jesus never allowed His faith to become a battleground with the Pharisees. Some people treat religion as a debate. But for us Catholics, faith should draw us into deeper relationships—with God and with others. Our problem is that we have a shrinking heart. When we see the problems of other people, we say, “That’s their problem, not mine. What God wants is for us to be overwhelmed by His power and not depend on our own. He wants us to get out of our shrinking heart syndrome and care for other people. ## 2026-06-06 — Anonymous

“For they have all contributed from their surplus wealth, but she, from her poverty, has 16-17, 22 contributed all she had, her whole livelihood.” – A bishop who lived around 300 A.D. was known for his extraordinary generosity. He chose to remain anonymous in all his good works. The story goes that there were three poor sisters whose father was unable to afford dowries for their marriage. This left them vulnerable to a life of hardship. The bishop, moved with pity, decided to help them by silently tossing a bag of gold coins in their house in the middle of the night. He did this for each of the daughters without letting the family know. This act of silent generosity was done by someone we now know as Saint Nicholas. His life is the inspiration for our modern-day Santa Claus. One of the lessons of today’s Gospel is that we can help people without the need to be recognized. Our motivation for helping others should be based on making their life better and not making ourselves famous. Doing good is about loving others, not ourselves. Alvin Fabella

## 2026-06-22 — Eye Problem

“Why worry about a speck in your friend’s 2 eye when you have a log in your own?” My dad was an optometrist. As a kid, I would have eye checkups with him in his clinic. At times, we may have “eye problems” in the form of being judgmental toward others. This is often caused by the lens of shame. We have an inner judge that tells us, “You’re ugly,” “You’re not good enough,” or “You don’t belong.” So when we shame ourselves, we shame others too. Shame leads us to judge ourselves harshly and we become judgmental toward others. So, we need to take off the lens of shame and get rid of our inner judge. Today, Jesus invites you to stop judging yourself. Stop judging others. Don’t take God’s seat. Don’t even borrow it, because you’re a horrible judge. Let God be your judge, because He’s merciful and wise beyond measure. Mike Viñas

## 2026-06-25 — Don’T Let The

### Reflection

“Then I will tell them plainly, ‘I never knew you. Away from me, you evildoers!’” When my long-time friend stopped replying to me, I thought she was just busy. Random chats that take us days to reply, meetups three to four times a year—we’re comfortable with our low-maintenance friendship. Maybe too comfortable. Months passed and still nothing. I believe the fallout didn’t just happen overnight. We slowly stopped doing simple things that once bonded us. We stopped building connections, then we started to drift apart. It got me thinking of my other relationships, especially with God. The hardest part of my Christian walk is daily prayer. To make time, sit still, and not let my mind wander even just for 10 minutes. Every now and then, I try to pick up my daily prayer habit, which normally lasts only a week. The motivation wears off. Months go by and I will try to return to my habit. I fall off, then I start over. But I keep on going. Even if I fail countless times, I keep on trying. I might not fully know God’s heart on this side of life, but I want Him to have full access to mine. Rinabelle Cabagyo 

### Prayer

Jesus, open my heart to Your friendship. St. Prosper of Aquitaine,

### Saint of the Day

St. Prosper of Aquitaine

## 2026-06-26 — God Can, But . . .

### Reflection

“Sir, if you want to, you can make me clean.” 2 – The man with the skin disease said, “If you want. . . .” If you want, you can heal me. If you want, you can use me. If you want, you can make a miracle. We can learn from his humility and tentative tone. It’s not a demand, but a request. He may sound like he is pleading, but it’s with due respect and surrender. He seems open to other possibilities that may happen, despite his longing for healing. His posture is being at Jesus’ At times, we pray so hard to the point of begging for what we want. But ultimately, we need to bring our desires to Him with all humility and say, “If You want to, Lord, You can.” If God does not allow it, it doesn’t mean He refused. He can, but not yet. Or perhaps we need to persevere more. God can, but He wants us to change inwardly and grow in humility He can give, and He can will it. He can, if we deepen our resolve to radically surrender ourselves as He prepares us for the blessing. Ichel Alignay 

### Prayer

God, I know You can. Give me a humble posture and an openness to receive Your love and grace for me. St. Josemaria Escriva de Balaguer,

### Saint of the Day

St. Josemaria Escriva de Balaguer

## 2026-06-27 — Stop. Look. Listen.

### Reflection

“Lord, I am not worthy to have you come under my roof. But simply say the word and 18-19 my servant will be healed.” – When The Chosen, a TV series based on the Gospels, released a snippet of the scene with Gaius, the centurion, asking Jesus to heal his servant, it became my morning eye lubricant. I would be moved to tears every time I watched it because I have been struggling to have that kind of faith. How did the centurion build that much trust in Jesus? It must not have been an instant conversion for a Gentile like him. The centurion stopped being a Roman soldier to observe Jesus. He looked at His miracles. He listened to His sermons. In real life, I need to go through the same process. Stop. Stop the busyness to pray, to meditate. Look—read the Bible and books on faith journeys like Sabbath and Didache. Listen to the Scriptures at Mass, sermons, and inspiring talks. I still have a long way to go before my faith is as strong as the centurion’s. But I believe if I continue to stop, look, and listen to Jesus, I’ll get there eventually. Lella M. Santiago 

### Prayer

My Jesus, I know I am not worthy because of my little faith. Thank You for answering my prayers despite that. St. Cyril of Alexandria, bishop and Doctor of the Church,

## 2026-06-28 — Hard To Love

### Reflection

Whoever does not take up his cross and 2 follow after me is not worthy of me. Whoever finds his life will lose it, and whoever loses his life for my sake will find it. – “Lord, if You really love me, why are You asking me to love this person? It’s so hard!” Have you ever questioned Jesus’ words and example because you find yourself confronted by your limitations? I didn’t even have to go far. I look at my kid and I feel inadequate as a father. I know some parents here can relate. But even as I am consumed by the feeling of defeat and desperation, looking at my child also fills my heart with so much love and compassion. I find myself telling God, “Lord, help my inability.” And I find renewed strength to continue to love my kid just one more hour. Kidding aside, I find that when God compels us to love, He is asking us a question. “Do you love Me more than your current comfort? Do you trust that I can love beyond your capabilities? Can we do this together?” God invites you to be His prophet to the people around you—in word and in life. Niko Capucion 

### Prayer

Lord, help my inability. Be my strength in my weakness, my courage in the midst of my fear, my firm footing in all the uncertainty. Saint Irenaeus, bishop and martyr,

## 2026-06-29 — You Are Free

### Reflection

Then Peter. . . said, “Now I know for certain that [the] Lord sent his angel and rescued me. . .” – For years, I was imprisoned by a negative self-image. I became wary of others after the people I trust betrayed me, I was sensitive to criticism, I would always compare myself to others, and I couldn’t make even small decisions. Things changed when I attended The Feast. My boyfriend then—now my husband—invited me to join a small sharing group and encouraged me to serve and meet new friends. I was skeptical, but slowly, I felt God’s love through the people around me. I felt free—after long years of doubt and hiding. Just as the angel miraculously freed Peter, my husband and co-servants were angels sent by God so I could live and serve to glorify Him. Now, our little family of four serve at The Feast Carmona, praying to be used by His love to free others too. Do you feel imprisoned by your bad habits and sins? God sends angels in the form of your family members, friends, and strangers to free you. Join a community like The Feast and know that, like Peter, the Church prays for your freedom. Love Cosio 

### Prayer

Lord, You show me Your lavish love through the people around me. May I never take them for granted. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Solemnity of Saints Peter

## 2026-06-30 — Faithful And True

### Reflection

He said to them, “Why are you terrified, O you ; 4:11-12 of little faith?” Then he got up, rebuked the winds and the sea, and there was great calm. – Stomach churning. Knocking knees. Clammy hands. I’m sure you’ve been there too. Most of us will accept that we’ve been terrified at one time or another. Personally, fear makes me turn to God as Father, Savior, and Friend. Ultimately, it makes me turn to Him as my End. Because the things that really terrify me involve loss of control and death, whether it’s physical, material, or psychological. So when I’m afraid, I discern His will on the matter and imagine giving myself to Him. Then I worship in faith, trusting in His Being a Christian doesn’t make us immune from fear. But it does provide us with a positive antidote: Our Great God. Let’s allow Him to rise up in our hearts for He will calm the storm. He will win, if we let Him. After all, He is not just Father. He is also King of kings, Lord of lords, and Prince of Peace. And most of all, He is faithful and true. Joyce Roa 

### Prayer

Lord, I believe. Help my unbelief. Thank You for Your untiring patience with me. Make me stronger through my fearful situations. The First Martyrs of the Holy Roman Church,

## 2026-07-01 — A Skull On Your Desk

### Reflection

Seek good, not evil, that you may live. – I once had a retreat in a monastery. I had a chance to visit a monk’s bedroom, and what I saw was minimalism to the absolute extreme. A small bed, a desk, a chair, a crucifix on the wall, and two solitary candles. But there was something unnerving: a human skull sitting on the desk. I learned that it was part of a practice called memento mori, Latin for “Remember you must die.” The skull was a reminder that death is just around the corner. It helped the monks make wise decisions. To live in the present moment. To love with all their heart. For, who knows, it might be their last day today. It also helps clarify their values, let go of attachments, and see what truly matters in life. Half the year is over and it’s a good time to recollect. Without having to put a skull on your desk, I invite you to find a way to remember your death. For some, it could be by praying for the dead; for others, it could be by visiting a cemetery once in a while. Simply put, find a way to remember that life is short, so love God and neighbor. Bo Sanchez 

### Prayer

Father, thank You for the gift of life. While I am still alive, help me to love You and my neighbor with all my heart. Help me to do good always. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Junipero Serra

## 2026-07-02 — Coming Close

### Reflection

When Jesus saw their faith, he said to the paralytic, “Courage, child, your sins are forgiven.” – It’s true that when you advance in age, you slowly transition to living a simple life. Some things are no longer as important to you, so you let go of them. I don’t frequent the malls anymore, I eat food in moderation, I spend more time in prayer and reading, and I no longer wear fancy clothes. On the outside, it looks like I’m living a simple life. But behind all that simplicity, there are still rubbish that I find hard to get rid of. Sin still clings to me. After confession, I would feel OK, knowing that I am forgiven. But the feeling of being forgiven lingers only for a few days and I would find myself back to where I was. I eventually fall again. I found it difficult to pray for forgiveness because I felt ashamed of what I did. But now, I realize that every time I fall into sin, Jesus is always ready to forgive. Even if you approach Jesus like a paralytic on a stretcher, He is ready to forgive no matter how impossible your situation is. Edwin Marcelo 

### Prayer

Lord, cleanse me of all my sins and forsake me not. Help me in my unbelief. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Ariston and companions

## 2026-07-03 — When You Feel Left Out

### Reflection

“Blessed are those who have not seen and yet have believed.” – Whenever I scroll through social media, it feels like everyone else is Apostle moving forward. People are thriving in their careers, traveling the world, and settling into their dream homes. Meanwhile, I feel stuck— waiting, wondering if God sees me too. It’s hard not to feel left behind. Have you ever felt that way? Thomas probably did. His friends had just witnessed something life-changing—Jesus is alive! But Thomas wasn’t there. He missed it. And while they rejoiced, he wrestled with doubt. He wanted Jesus to meet him too. And Jesus met him in that longing. A week later, Jesus appeared—not to shame Thomas, but to meet him right where he was. He held out His hands and side, and showed Thomas I believe Jesus does the same for us. The blessings we wait for will come in time, but Jesus doesn’t make us wait for Him. He is already near, meeting us in our doubts. Even when life feels like it’s passing us by, with Him, we are never left behind. Tisha Alyssa Sanchez 

### Prayer

Jesus, when I feel left out, help me trust that You see me. Strengthen my faith and remind me that You are always near. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Feast of Saint Thomas,

## 2026-07-04 — God’S New Work

### Reflection

“And no one puts new wine into old wineskins.” – NLT Bo Sanchez often encourages us, Feast builders, to always be open to new things—to think of innovative ways to deliver God’s Word, to build better systems for growing our ministry, to use technology to advance the Gospel, and to listen to the next generation. At first, going live on Facebook, YouTube, and Instagram was intimidating, but it worked. We tried building small groups via Zoom, WhatsApp, and even Discord, and reached more people—even beyond the Philippines. We made our Feasts more interactive while maintaining a relational approach and encouraged our leaders to bring faith to the workplace. We embraced the idea that The Feast is not just about physical venues but a movement. I am also finishing my studies in Theology and Family Ministry because I want to stay updated on how to best love my family and help other families as well. Jesus calls us to receive the “new wine” of His Kingdom. To do so, we must prepare our hearts as new wineskins, ready to hold His blessings and teachings. Jan Silan 

### Prayer

My Jesus, help me to be humble and open to Your guidance. Grant me the wisdom to know when to hold on and when to let go. Amen.

St. Elizabeth of Portugal
