## 2026-07-05 — Purpose Over Pleasure

### Reflection

If by the spirit you put to death the deeds of the body, you will live. – Binge all day on K-dramas versus having a regular prayer time. Doomscrolling versus learning a new skill. Shop online for stuff you don’t need versus generosity to a ministry. In a world where social media and other platforms continuously tempt us with instant gratification and material pleasures, it’s challenging to stay grounded in values that lead to lasting joy. We’re addicted to “simple pleasures” that make us neglect the deeper satisfaction that comes from aligning ourselves with our life’s real purpose. To win this constant battle, we have to set our minds on the things of the Spirit. By prioritizing discipline, love, and service over momentary pleasures, we create an environment where our families, teammates, and communities can thrive. “An idle mind is the workshop of the devil,” so we need to develop everyday habits that lead to success in our lives. By filling our day with activities that matter, we will experience fulfillment beyond the surface-level distractions of the world and build a life grounded on God’s eternal purpose. Rex Mendoza 

### Prayer

Dear Lord, guide and help me schedule my days and design my life to avoid the fleeting temptations of the world and the flesh. Amen.

## 2026-07-06 — Fake News And Bashers

### Reflection

“Go away! The girl is not dead but sleeping.” And they ridiculed him. – Long before the advent of Internet and social media, I had a classmate who constantly maligned me. One day, on my birthday, he went around spreading fake news that I was terminally ill. We were both vying for honors in class and, in hindsight, his bullying was a way to distract me from focusing on my studies. Today, our exposure to social media makes us vulnerable to bashing or becoming a hater ourselves. It’s tempting to respond defensively when we get negative comments or to criticize those we don’t necessarily agree with. But we must never lose sight of who we are—a child of God. The only opinion that matters is God’s. Jesus was also bashed during His time. He was ridiculed throughout His ministry. But He stayed on course as the Son of God. He remained focused on His mission to save us. To grow as a Christian is to learn how to respond appropriately when we are criticized. Jane Gonzales-Rauch 

### Prayer

Dear Lord, fill me with Your peace and compassion that I may not be swayed by negativity around me. Guide me with Your wisdom so I may respond to haters with grace, understanding, and forgiveness. Amen.

## 2026-07-07 — Called To Love Daily

### Reflection

He said to his disciples, “The harvest is great, but the workers are few. So pray to the Lord who is in charge of the harvest; ask him to send more workers into his fields.” – Matthew‬ ‭9‬:‭37-38,‬ ‭NLT A decade ago, I had the beautiful privilege of serving the youth in our community. Being part of the youth ministry and knowing that I was doing a significant work for the Kingdom of God truly felt fulfilling. Fast forward to today, I am no longer serving as a youth minister. I am now a wife, a homemaker, and a momma to three beautiful children. While I may no longer have the role or title of being a youth leader, I know that God’s calling for me to love and share the Gospel continues. If anything, my harvest field just looks different now. The daily acts of loving, praying for, sheltering, and serving my husband and my three children are now my new version of discipleship. It’s easy to think that serving within church structures is the only “real” work that we can do for the Kingdom of God. The truth is, we can share God’s love wherever we are. We are all called to work for the Lord by sharing His love daily. Chai Gamo

Father, direct our steps and reveal the work You have prepared for me. Guide me to those You’re calling me to lead closer to You. Amen.

Blessed Maria Romero Meneses

## 2026-07-08 — Unchurched

“Go rather to the lost sheep of the house of Israel.” – When I became a Feast builder and preacher of the Light of Jesus Family, we were given the mandate to minister to the unchurched—those who no longer hear Mass or receive the sacraments. So, that’s We opened up live Feasts in theaters, malls, hotels, and public centers where we praise, worship, and give talks about God’s Word. We also celebrate the Holy Eucharist. Moreover, we came up with Feast Lights, which are small gatherings in offices, schools, coffee shops, or in homes where people watched videos of Feast talks. We let God meet them where Now, there are hundreds of Feasts throughout the country and around the world, with thousands and thousands of Feasters attending. In fact, the goal is to “make one million Indeed, the call to seek out to the “lost sheep” continues, most especially in today’s world. Alvin Barcelona

## 2026-07-09 — Recall

### Reflection

“When Israel was a child, I loved him, and I called my son out of Egypt. But the more I called to him, the farther he moved from me.” Here’s a dog meme I like. “My dog: unlimited food, water, shelter, and love in my home.” Front door opens. My dog: (sprinting like crazy away from home). Fur parents would laugh—and lament—at this truth. No matter how secure their homes are, the moment dogs see an open door, they flee. They don’t know that the world outside is harsh. Unlike at home, food outside is scarce. Compared to inside, love outside is scarce. Scarier than them dashing out the door is when they don’t come back when called. It either runs further, stays put, or chases something else. This leads to the added stress of having to chase your dog, fearing it might get hurt, stolen, or lost. Just as fleeing dogs need to have good recall to return when called by their masters, so do wandering hearts need to respond when their Master calls them home. Have you run away from God? He lovingly calls you now. Time to run back home. Toh Relova 

### Prayer

Lord, my safest place is in Your arms. May I run back home to You now. St. Joseph Zhang Dapeng,

### Saint of the Day

St. Joseph Zhang Dapeng

## 2026-07-10 — Vip

### Reflection

I have humbled him, but I will take note of him. – I grew up in the world of showbiz where an actor is treated as VIP. But it’s the exact opposite when you choose to serve the Lord. I experienced having to eat leftover fishbones from the participants of a Life in the Spirit Seminar because there was no food left for the servants. We had several K5 Preachers concerts abroad where our dressing rooms were in the kitchen or a gym stockroom. There was even a time when the dressing area was right on the stage, with just a thin curtain to cover us. But one unforgettable service was when I was asked to lead worship in a province where Bo Sanchez was the main speaker. After the rehearsal, I was ushered into a room where I was supposed to take a shower. It turned out to be the janitor’s room, with a faucet almost on the floor, a pail, and a mop beside it. The lights weren’t even working. Backstage, before the event started, I complained to Bo about my ordeal. I was expecting him to empathize with me. Instead, he said, “Now, you are a true servant!” A VIP means to be a Very Inspiring Person. Adrian Panganiban

Lord, make me a servant, humble and meek. Saint Amalberga,

Saint Amalberga

## 2026-07-11 — Be Brave For Them

### Reflection

“Don’t be intimidated. Eventually everything is going to be out in the open, and everyone will know how things really are. So don’t hesitate to go public now.” – MSG God has called me to speak about chastity, or more specifically, recommitting oneself to purity. It’s not a popular choice in our day and age when it’s common for unmarried couples to sleep together or live in. Even without a commitment, people get physically intimate with each other. I’ve done my fair share of disobedience in this area, but I’m glad I allowed the Lord to intervene and change my ways. Purity is the best gift I’ve given myself. Because I discovered my true worth and dignity, I didn’t waste time in wrong relationships. Most importantly, I discovered my purpose. Chastity leads to I also speak and write about father wounds and generational healing, which is also not easy as it touches on family issues. But when I think about children who suffer in broken families, I’m moved to speak God’s truth to youth and singles who will start future families to prevent further damage to individuals. There was never a time when God didn’t make His power felt. Marianne Mencias

Jesus, empower me to be Your mouthpiece to bring Your healing to this broken world. Saint Benedict, abbot,

## 2026-07-12 — Let Jesus Grow

### Reflection

“But blessed are your eyes, because they see, and your ears, because they hear.” – I waited for my son Jacob at the school gate after his classes, but he was nowhere to be found. I was getting impatient when he came out drenched in sweat. He said he watered his plant at the back of the school where they had their Science projects. Almost daily, he would visit and water his plant expecting that one day it will grow into a big tree. Between recess and playtime, he would check and pull out weeds to keep them from growing Jesus is the seed that wants to take root in our lives. It requires utmost care and attention every day. We need to water ourselves with His Words, encounter Him in the Eucharist, and spend precious moments in the Blessed Sacrament. Pull out the weeds that keep His roots from growing in our hearts through the Sacrament of Reconciliation. Let’s be intentional in letting Jesus grow in our life. Randel Joseph Serrano

## 2026-07-13 — What’S My Cup?

### Reflection

“And whoever gives only a cup of cold water . . .” – –11:1 As a senior citizen, I’ve witnessed big and small stuff. I used to think the big moments are what count. Now, things may have slowed down a bit, but it’s the small stuff that gives a more powerful punch. At home, I may not be the strong handyman who can lift, push, pull, and hammer with might. But it’s taking out the garbage, lifting the toilet seat, doing the dishes every now and then, and laughing at the jokes. While running my lighting company, I can get caught up in deadlines, with the bottom line and irate clients. But it’s not the big contracts that define me. It’s how I treat with kindness the people who are the bloodline and skeleton of my company. It’s showing my gratitude for their efforts. In the community, it’s not about grand donations or volunteering for every cause. It’s the small interactions, the friendly wave to the security guard at the gate, and a chat with the taho vendor about the planets and the stars. These little things add up. They’re my “cup”—offered not for show, but from the heart. Rene Espinosa 

### Prayer

God. Accept, oh Lord, these small acts of mine that I choose to bless You through. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Henry II

## 2026-07-14 — Emergency Novena

### Reflection

“Remain calm and be unafraid. Do not let your courage fail.” – NCB Being a panicky person, I find it hard to follow our key verse. So, how do I boost my courage? I pray St. Teresa of Calcutta’s emergency “flying novena” to Mama Mary. I’ve prayed it ever since whenever I fear an impending and immediate disaster. OK, I admit, “disaster” is relative. It has ranged from my son’s delayed connecting flight, to fears that someone in the family had COVID, to not having enough to pay BIR’s ridiculously high estate tax, and many other problems. Believe me, I can’t remember a time when this novena failed. Our Mother holds the backdoor open for me to reach her Son. The emergency novena is simply the Memorare said nine consecutive times. Saint Teresa prayed this when time was of the essence and trouble would already happen before she could finish a nine-day novena. The Memorare helps me do it afraid because my powerful backer is our Savior’s own Mother. Lella M. Santiago

St. Kateri Tekakwitha

## 2026-07-15 — Rest Easy

### Reflection

“By my own power I have done it, and by my wisdom, for I am shrewd. – As I write this reflection, the Los Angeles fires have been top of the news for several days now. A line from a news story said: “It’s also obvious that wildfire is no respecter of person, profession, or income. Some of the most valuable real estate in the country have been reduced to ash” (Daily Citizen, January 9, 2025). Feeling helpless and without control can be frustrating. The Kenneth fires started where my only brother lives and they were ordered to evacuate. I begged friends and community for prayers. By the grace of God, the order was lowered to a warning after a few hours. During my prayer time, I sensed the Lord say, “Are you concerned about your brother? Even more so am I.” Then He gave me a sense of reassurance and Our desire for control can only be abated if we learn to get to know and experience the One who is truly in full control. And when we do and realize that He is totally for us and loves us, then we can rest easy. Joyce Roa 

### Prayer

Dearest Lord, remind me to stop and come to You when I try to assert my power and control. Let me experience Your love so I may rest easy. Amen.

## 2026-07-16 — Earthquake!

### Reflection

“Come to me . . . I will give you rest.” “Lord, have mercy! Save us!” I prayed as the ground shook beneath me. Mud and rocks fell with the roar of thunder. The dark clouds and gushing wind came with the pouring of rain and hail. It seemed like the end of the world. I was alone at home in Baguio during the devastating 1990 earthquake. I went out of the house barefoot, wet under the rain, worried and afraid for my family. I invoked the intercession of the Blessed Mother, Our Lady of Mount Carmel, whose feast was on that fateful day, July 16. Eventually, my family arrived home and we camped outside our dilapidated house, under which a faultline passed through. The concrete front wall of our house fell and only one section was left standing. Then it hit me. The remaining wall was directly behind me as I stood in front of the house while everything collapsed. God heard my prayer, and I was saved that day. Sometimes, we think there is no hope with our problems. That God doesn’t hear our prayers when things seem to fall apart. Friend, call upon the Lord. Trust that He is working behind the scenes for your good. Jonathan Yogawin 

### Prayer

Jesus, I trust in You. Our Lady of Mount Carmel,

### Saint of the Day

Our Lady of Mount Carmel

## 2026-07-17 — When God Rewrites

### Reflection

This will be the sign for you from 21-22, 7-8 the Lord that the Lord will carry out the word he has spoken. – When I was in my early ’20s, I dreamed of giving my parents a condo. I thought that would be a great way to give back for all their sacrifices for me. But they were aging, retired or almost, and I was nowhere near affording a condo downpayment. Nevertheless, I wrote it in my Novena to God’s Love booklet. Fast forward, my parents bought a retirement home in a peaceful place in Cavite with their money. I looked at that dream in my novena and thought, “Oh well, it’s probably a wrong dream.” But during the transaction with the bank, my parents asked if I could be the primary loan owner. I was young with a stable job so the bank would prefer me versus senior citizens. So, I submitted all my documents and we were able to purchase their retirement home. I scratched my head and prayed, “So, this is Your version of me getting my parents a home!” I still find it funny to this day. Our dreams are there because God planted them in our hearts and He fulfills His promise. But not always in our version. Because His is always better. Tintin Taton

Lord, You answer all my prayers in the best version possible and in the most perfect time. Teach me to trust in Your ways. Saint Alexis,

Saint Alexis

## 2026-07-18 — We Hear But

### Reflection

Aware of this, Jesus withdrew from that place. A large crowd followed him, and he healed all who were ill. – NIV I love podcasts. Whether for education or entertainment, I find it convenient to listen to an episode while I’m out walking, driving, washing the dishes, or working out. I usually have it played on speakers or earphones. I am such a huge consumer of podcasts that I decided to publish one myself! Because of how much I enjoy this kind of content, I became aware that I have to be more mindful of how I consume it. After all, anything extreme, even if it starts out good, eventually becomes bad. I need to keep myself from filling every moment of silence with another person’s voice or Our world today is full of noise and stimuli. It’s so easy to get distracted and not make time for solitude. Like Jesus, we need to relearn how to withdraw from the world every now and then so we may hear His voice and receive His grace. Mon Reyes

## 2026-07-19 — The Wheat Wait!

### Reflection

“Let them grow together until harvest; then at Wisdom 12:13, 16-19 harvest time I will say to the harvesters, ‘First collect the weeds and tie them in bundles for 15-16 burning; but gather the wheat into my barn.’” – Why did the wise master stop the workers from pulling up the weeds on the spot? Why did he make them wait until the wheat and the weeds grow together at harvest season? That’s because wheat and weeds look alike. The difference only becomes recognizable when the wheat bears grains. It’s the “fruit” that distinguishes the wheat from the weed. I can’t count the number of times that impatience sabotaged my relationships. I immediately label people as “weeds” and yank them out of my life because I don’t like them. But God blessed me with beautiful friendships where I learned that no one is pure evil. If I wait patiently, I get pleasantly surprised with their good side. A friend may disappoint me big time, but if I choose to remember all the good things the person has done, my disappointment shrinks. I’ve failed others, too, and I’m happy they didn’t consider me May I not be too quick to judge. With patience, may I clearly see the wheat among the weeds. Jon Escoto 

### Prayer

Lord, may I see people as You see them. Amen.

Saints Justa and Rufina

## 2026-07-20 — In The Dark

### Reflection

“Just as Jonah was in the belly of the whale three days and three nights . . .” – I was by myself in an office room when the lights went out. Luckily, the exit was nearby and I groped my way in total darkness until I felt the door handle. Scary. But those minutes of blindness were nothing compared to Adrian’s In February 2025, a news clip showed Adrian Simancas, 23, rafting in Chilean waters. From behind, he and his inflatable boat were suddenly engulfed inside a blue-white “wall” and disappeared. He quickly resurfaced and realized he had been swallowed by a humpback whale! Thankfully, the whale spat him out after three seconds. News articles linked Adrian’s and Jonah’s names together. We don’t have to physically suffocate or be without sight to feel lost. The trials we hurdle lead to uncertainty and fear. We feel helpless as we call out into the void. We may believe we aren’t worthy of blessings or forgiveness because we are sinners. But God is merciful. He sent His only Son to save us. When we pray in gratitude, in repentance, and in sincere faith—our Father fills us with the light of His love. Gina J. Verdolaga 

### Prayer

Dear Lord, thank You for allowing me to feel Your personal presence in my daily life. St. Joseph of Barsabas,

### Saint of the Day

St. Joseph of Barsabas

## 2026-07-21 — Grandma And

Shepherd your people with your staff. Grace, Tala, Jack, Pana, and Kaya—our five grandchildren—are God’s gifts to Raul and me. They’re the flock entrusted to us. As we reach our sunset years, we recognize the immense responsibility and privilege of guiding them with love, wisdom, and faith. calls us to shepherd God’s people with care, just as a shepherd leads his flock to green pastures. We are called to nurture, protect, and guide our grandchildren, not with rigid authority but with a staff of grace, patience, and understanding. Life’s journey is filled with uncertainties, yet we trust that God’s wisdom will direct our steps to produce the fruits of the Holy Spirit in each of them. Parenting and grandparenting are sacred callings. We pray that our love and day-to-day witnessing lead them closer to God’s heart. May they grow with strong roots in faith and confidence in God’s unfailing love, knowing they are deeply loved. Mari Sison Garcia

## 2026-07-23 — What Do You See?

### Reflection

“But blessed are your eyes because they see, and your ears because they hear.” I started vlogging at just eight years old—capturing everything from trips I took, food I loved, crafts I made, and even songs I liked to sing. Now at 17, after nine years of recording my life and the moments unfolding before my eyes, vlogging remains one of the biggest parts of who I am. It’s my absolute favorite hobby—and the way I share the truth of God’s love online. In today’s Gospel, Jesus tells His curious disciples, “But blessed are your eyes because they see, and your ears because they hear.” I imagine God saying those words to me—that I’m blessed to see what I’ve seen, to hear what I’ve heard, and most of all, to remember His glory in the everyday moments I capture on camera. You, too, are so blessed, my friend! Are there glory-filled moments in your life you never want to forget? Things you’ve seen that shout of His goodness? Capture them. Share them. Hold on to them. Because you never know how many hearts they’ll touch. Helene Gogna 

### Prayer

Lord, You bless me every day. Open my eyes to the great things You are doing. Don’t let me miss any of them. Saint Bridget, religious,

## 2026-07-24 — Identity Crisis

### Reflection

“But the seed sown on rich soil is the one who hears the word and understands it, who indeed bears fruit and yields a hundred or sixty or thirtyfold.” – For a long time, I struggled with my identity. Sometimes, I felt like I was split in half—one served the Lord while the other served the demands of the world. There were days when I’d be ashamed to come before God, believing I was an imposter who didn’t deserve forgiveness. That’s when I realized that if that was my image of the Lord, then maybe I didn’t know Him as well as I thought. Picking up the Bible, summarizing parables, or reciting verses is one thing. But to read the Scripture, listen to it, and live it is another. To understand God’s Word is to know that He loves us unconditionally. He embraces us as we are, and we are encouraged to do the same with others. We need to live the Word not in a double life but as our whole life. When His Word becomes the center, our identity is found in our Creator. We no longer have to hide. May that part of us that serves the world’s demands transform into serving the world in His name. Gemma Casimsiman 

### Prayer

Father, grant me the wisdom to understand Your Word and the heart to live it. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Kinga of Poland

## 2026-07-25 — Through The Fire

### Reflection

We are hard pressed on every side, but 2 not crushed; perplexed, but not in despair; persecuted, but not abandoned; struck down, but not destroyed. – 2 NIV Hard pressed. Perplexed. Struck down. That’s what it felt like walking through cancer’s fire. Each day brought a fresh round of pain harder than the last. The physical and emotional turmoil was exhausting. I sat alone in the chemo room, overwhelmed by the weight of my diagnosis, and pray, “God, I need You to carry me through this.” It was an honest prayer of letting go of control. Then I felt a comforting peace, lifting the burden I held on to tightly. Things didn’t suddenly become OK, but I felt an assurance that God was watching over me. His peace settled where uncertainty once reigned. Cancer led me to a place of peace, not defined by the absence of pain but by the presence of God. It became an opportunity to draw closer to Him and deepen my faith. Faith filled the space between fear and hope, not as a means of escape from my reality but a reminder that I wasn’t walking through the fire alone, assuring me that I was loved, cherished, never abandoned. Marie G. Ferrer 

### Prayer

Father, thank You for walking with me through every trial, for being a constant presence in times of suffering and uncertainty. Help me to trust You more deeply and to find peace in Your love that goes beyond my own understanding. Amen.

Feast of Saint James, Apostle

## 2026-07-26 — What Would You Ask?

### Reflection

In Gibeon the Lord appeared to Solomon 1 in a dream at night. God said: “Whatever you ask I shall give you.” – 1 It’s the stuff fairy tales are made of. Except this time, it’s not some magical fairy but God Himself—the Creator of the entire universe! Solomon was clear-sighted when he asked for wisdom to rule his people. And because God was so pleased with his request, He threw in everything else—riches, glory, and a long life! This reading reminded me of another Bible story where a powerful king promised to give a person anything she wanted. Herodias’s daughter danced and pleased King Herod so much that he offered to give her anything she wanted. She could have asked for half the kingdom, a hundred more wishes, or a lifetime supply of Botox. Instead, she asked for the head of John the Baptist, who had angered her mother for criticizing her parents’ illicit marriage (cf. ). What a waste of great favor! Saints Joachim and Anne were childless, but when they prayed and were blessed with a baby, they didn’t squander the favor given them. They raised Mary to be godly and eventually give her monumental yes to God. Rissa Singson Kawpeng 

### Prayer

Lord, I ask not for what I want but what You desire for me. Amen.

Blessed Virgin Mary

## 2026-07-28 — Cry And Cry

### Reflection

My eyes hurt from crying all day. I cried before going to sleep, I cried when I woke up realizing my son was no longer with me. There were times when I didn’t want to get out of bed and just marinate in my grief. Some people told me to stop crying, to be strong, but I think being strong is overrated. I couldn’t just keep all my grief inside. It would be like a pressure cooker waiting to explode. I was advised to feel my feelings because what I could feel, I Grief never goes away. It evolves and takes a different shape and weight. From something so heavy you can hardly carry—like a balikbayan box—to a hand-carry luggage you can pull around, to eventually a light, small purse. In time, my tears have subsided but not completely. Yet, I have peace in my heart. With or without tears, my beloved is always with me, never forgotten, forever loved. Cheri Roberto-Daco

## 2026-07-31 — Come Prepared

They held it outside the town hall, in open air, so “God can hear our voices loudly and directly.” The village folk arrived on the agreed day and time. They formed a prayer circle and were ready to pray. Then a woman came with her young daughter, carrying a huge umbrella. His neighbors asked her, “Why are you bringing an umbrella in the middle of a drought?” She replied, “Aren’t we praying for rain? I came prepared.” Jesus didn’t do miracles in his hometown because of their unbelief. He did not blame them. He simply stated the obvious: faith allows miracles to happen. God’s power is sometimes diminished in our disbelief. Not that He is less powerful, but because our faith ushers in God’s power. Our faith is a key that unlocks His miracles. We need to open our hearts and trust. Because faith is not just believing. It is also preparing yourself for God’s miracles that are coming. Boggs Burbos

## 2026-08-12 — Loving Intervention

### Reflection

“If your brother or sister sins, go and point out ; 10:18-19, 22 their fault, just between the two of you. If they listen to you, you have won them over.” – In her book, A Story of a Soul, Saint Therese writes about correcting a novice for a weakness. Therese prayed for guidance because she didn’t want to hurt the sister but wanted to correct her lovingly. We are also called to watch out for each other’s well-being. Just as we wouldn’t hesitate to call out a friend who’s about to fall off a cliff or crossing a street with an oncoming car, we don’t want to endanger their souls if they are committing sin. Jesus counsels us to point out the fault just between us. He then encourages us not to give up if they refuse to listen, but bring in one or two trustworthy witnesses to help. And finally, to bring in an elder or priest for the final effort. This process of fraternal correction is a stark contrast to what people do on social media when they call out a person’s fault for all the world to see. As children of God, we do not shame our brethren, but we correct them out of love, through God’s way, for their well-being. Marjorie Duterte

Dear Jesus, help me to love my neighbor and, if necessary, to correct them with love. St. Jane Frances de Chantal,

## 2026-08-14 — I Dos And I Don’Ts

### Reflection

For this reason a man shall leave his father and mother and be joined to his wife, and the (or ) two shall become one flesh. – What does it mean to be married to someone? In my 12 years of marriage to Kristel, I have a simple answer: Marriage is a vow and a conscious commitment of what you can do and what you cannot do. When I said “I do” to Kristel at God’s altar, I knew what I was committing to. “I do” to loving her forever. “I do” to dying to myself daily. “I do” to providing for her needs. At the same time, I was also saying “I don’t.” “I don’t” to having romantic feelings for other women. “I don’t” to prioritizing only my dreams. “I don’t” to deliberately hurting her. This works the same way with God. As believers, we are married to Him. He entered into a covenant with us. So, on one side, we’re saying “I do” to God. “I do” to worshiping Him. “I do” to loving Him. But on the other side, we’re also saying “I don’t.” “I don’t” to having other gods. “I don’t” to hurting Him. Always remember your commitment to the Lord. He will never forget His commitment to you. Audee Villaraza 

### Prayer

Lord, give me the discipline to be a true disciple. Teach me to be faithful to You. St. Maximilian Mary Kolbe, priest and martyr,

## 2026-08-15 — Gentle Woman

### Reflection

“For he has looked upon his handmaid’s ; lowliness; behold, from now on will 12:1-6, 10 all ages call me blessed.” – ‭‭‭Luke‬ 1 Being in the exorcism ministry of the Archdiocese of Manila gives of the Blessed Virgin Mary me a front-seat view of how demons hate Mama Mary. They scream at the mention of her name. They would refer to her as “that woman.” One time, we placed the rosary on the possessed and the demon begged to have it removed because it was burning him. When asked by a famous American exorcist why they hate her so much, the demon replied that it’s because of her humility and obedience to God—a stark contrast to the devil’s pride and rebellion. Their pride cannot accept the fact that a humble woman has been elevated above them and has power over them. When Mary shows up in a session, they have no choice but to leave. Once, during a very difficult exorcism, our exorcist prayed to Mama Mary desperately. After a few minutes, the demon exclaimed, “You’re really so unfair! Look what you did. Now she’s here!” And the demon left. Aren’t we so blessed to have such a powerful mother? Ronna Singson Ledesma 

### Saint of the Day

Saint Alipius

## 2026-08-16 — The Cry Room

### Reflection

For my house shall be called a house of prayer for all peoples. – In our parish, there is a “cry room” where parents and guardians are encouraged to sit with their infants and toddlers who may cry or become rowdy during the Mass. We also have an announcement to keep the children from loitering around the church, especially in front of the altar. While I believe these are measures to preserve the conducive for prayer for the other churchgoers, I am bothered by the term “cry room.” I feel that we are rebuking children from becoming children when their innocence and authentic faith are what give hope to our suffering Church, with its rapidly dwindling congregation. As Cardinal Chito Tagle put it, “Which would you prefer—the noise of a church full of energetic or restless children running around or the sound of elderly people in coughing fits?” No offense meant for the seniors like me. Let’s “suffer” the little children. Rename the “cry room” into a “quiet room” where sounds of the innocent float up as prayers to the Lord who loves them. Tessa Mangahas 

### Prayer

Lord, listen to my restless cries as You listen to the cries of little children. St. Stephen of Hungary,

### Saint of the Day

St. Stephen of Hungary

## 2026-08-17 — Perfection Vs.

### Reflection

Jesus said, “If you want to be complete, go, sell what you own, and give the money to the 20, 21 poor. Then you will have treasure in heaven. And come follow me.” – CEB There are two ways of interpreting the meaning of “the best:” perfection and excellence. If you want to achieve perfection, then work hard and never settle for anything other than the top standard. Anything less than the utmost would be shameful. For me, this unchecked sense of perfection is often the basis of social- emotional dilemmas, like obsessive-compulsive disorder and neurosis. Swiss psychotherapist Carl Jung defined neurosis as “an escape from legitimate suffering.” I think it’s the adherence to peak standards of perfectionism that makes it damaging. On the contrary, excellence is not so process-oriented as it is results-oriented. It’s a product of diligence and hard work. American football coach Vince Lombardi rightly said, “Perfection is not attainable, but if we chase perfection we can catch excellence.” Excellence embraces legitimate suffering. Though some of the Bible versions differ—using perfect, complete, excellent—for sure, God is calling us to diligently cooperate with His grace to achieve excellence, especially in love. Obet Cabrillas 

### Prayer

Lord, I strive eagerly for the greatest spiritual gifts, the more excellent way of love (cf. 1 Corinthians 12:31). St. Clare of Montefalco,

### Saint of the Day

St. Clare of Montefalco

## 2026-08-18 — Forms Of Riches

### Reflection

“Again I say to you, it is easier for a camel to pass through the eye of a needle than for one who is rich to enter the kingdom of God.” 27-28, 30, 35-36 – One of the parish groups assigned to me has been around for decades. They take pride in their long history and previous accomplishments. However, during my first meeting with them, I did something that deviated from To my surprise, one of their leaders took me aside a week later to confront me. I was speechless and shocked. I felt like a child being scolded by his teacher for not following On another occasion, a member from the same group texted me a “simple reminder” to arrive on time and stay for the entire meeting. Again, this made me feel irresponsible despite the many efforts I’ve exerted. Riches in our key passage can refer to things other than money: our long-held traditions, power, prestige, longevity, etc. If we hold on to it excessively and obsessively, they can lead us away from the Kingdom of God. Fr. Migs Ramirez 

### Prayer

Father, I am sorry for the times I made my “riches” more important than You. May I desire to always seek You first. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Helena

## 2026-08-19 — Justice And Opportunity

### Reflection

They answered, “Because no one has hired us.” He said to them, “You too go into my vineyard.” – In my ministry with the Prison Mission, I began seeing the Gospels in the lens of the poor. No, the “5 p.m. workers” were not a bunch of lazy people sleeping all day and decided to work at the last hour. They really wanted to work. They were standing out there the “whole day.” But no one hired Who can these 5 p.m. workers be? The unwanted. The weak, disabled, and outcasts. Like Stephen, who isn’t a top candidate for job openings because he’s blind. Or Gary, who is ignored when employers learn he’s an ex-convict. Or Sajid, an uneducated Aeta who grew up in the mountains but makes just half the minimum wage. The 5 p.m. workers were paid the same amount as early-dawn starters because the landowner was a person of extravagant mercy and acted justly. My mother, Belen— my saint and my hero—also opened doors for education to many poor young souls. “All they want is a fair shot at life,” she believed. My friend, that’s a person of mercy, working for justice. Jon Escoto 

### Prayer

Jesus, fill my heart with the genuine spirit of Your mercy, so that I may act with justice to the person You send my way. Amen.

## 2026-08-20 — From Impossible

### Reflection

I will put my spirit in you and will see to it that you follow my laws and keep all the 18-19 commands I have given you. Do you ever feel like there are things that you want to change to yourself, but no matter how hard you try, it feels impossible? If yes, could it be that you have yet to fully invite the Holy Spirit into your situation? Today’s First Reading reminds us that it is God who replaces our hearts of stone. It is the Holy Spirit who moves us to obey His commands. On our own, we cannot overcome sin, change our ways, or live holy lives. We need God’s grace to do what’s pleasing in His eyes. No amount of willpower or goal- setting can truly transform us. We will fall short on our own. God alone can make the impossible possible. That’s why Jesus came to save and make us new. He doesn’t ask us to change by our strength; He offers His Spirit to work in us. If you’re struggling to follow God’s commands or long for change in your life, surrender it to Him. Ask for a fresh outpouring of His Holy Spirit. Let God do the impossible in your life! Teresa G. Dumadag

## 2026-08-21 — Challenge To Love

### Reflection

“And the second is like it: ‘Love your neighbor as yourself.’” – One of my biggest irritations is when people are late. I’ve learned from books and mentors that being late shows a lack of respect for others’ time. It damages your reputation, reflects poor management skills, and makes you appear unreliable and untrustworthy. A friend of mine struggles with this. She’s often 30 minutes to an hour late to her meetings. I’ve tried to explain how this affects her leadership, sharing my own experience since I, too, struggled with punctuality early in my career. While she still has occasional late moments, I’ve seen significant Instead of getting irritated, I’ve learned to choose kindness, patience, and understanding. After all, I’ve been there myself, and my mentor showed me the same patience to It’s easy to love those who treat us well, but Jesus challenges us to love even those we struggle with. Maine Ybiernas 

### Prayer

Lord, thank You for loving an imperfect person like me. Teach me that loving people is the best way to love You. Amen.

## 2026-08-22 — The Reason I Shout

### Reflection

“Therefore, do and observe all things whatsoever they tell you, but do not follow (or ) their example. For they preach but they do not practice.” – (or ) As head of our youth music ministry, I came up with 21 new rules which I strictly enforced. I believed of the Blessed Virgin Mary this frontliner ministry deserved the holiest of servants. But behind my militant leadership was guilt and shame for unconfessed impurity. Hypocrisy can lead to self-righteousness and unforgiveness, when oftentimes it’s because we haven’t Decades later, I’m still guilty of doing the same thing with my son. I come down hard on him for the very things I struggle with myself—being disorganized, failing to prioritize what matters, not getting ready on time, being late, eating poorly, or watching too much TV. I know I’m not a good example, so I raise my voice to drown out the loudness of my actions. As I write this, I am experiencing God’s conviction. Once again, I ask for His forgiveness and for the grace to forgive myself. I pray I would become a more gentle, kind, and compassionate father—one who leads with quiet action, not shouting words. George Tolentino Gabriel 

### Prayer

Lord, You always do what You say. Help me to be more like You in that way. St. Andrew the Scot,

St. Andrew the Scot

## 2026-08-23 — I Am Who I Am

### Reflection

“Who do you say I am?” – NIV “You are encouraged to avail of the special retirement package,” said the company representative. In other words, I should resign. I had mixed emotions. On one hand, I was grateful. I’ve been on prolonged medical leave due to a chronic illness exacerbated by cancer treatments. I knew my body could no longer cope with full-time work. I was thankful to receive monetary compensation that I wouldn’t otherwise get if I resigned. On the other hand, I dreaded the finality of leaving the workforce. My health had deteriorated to the point that I was housebound. I knew it was a permanent goodbye to productive leadership and entering a season of dependency. I grieved. But with the sadness was calm acceptance. Throughout my long-term illness, I had to restructure my self-identity according to my diminishing capabilities. In uncertainty, what assuaged me was knowing that, at my core, I remain unchanged, still made wonderfully in His image. In God’s eyes, we are always loved. And we fulfill a purpose no matter our circumstances in life. Cecil Lim

## 2026-08-25 — Be A Seeker, Not A Cynic

### Reflection

Therefore, brothers, stand firm and hold fast 2 to the traditions that you were taught, either 14-17 by an oral statement or by a letter of ours. – 2 One of the many things I love about serving in the youth ministry is the questions they ask. Like, “Why do we have to pray if God already knows what He’s going to do?” It’s beautiful because many of them honestly seek answers. It is easy to ask questions like these sarcastically, not to encourage discussion but to shut it down as if the question in itself is a statement. But those who genuinely seek ask questions to open doors, not close them. As we grow old, we stop asking questions like these. Sometimes, it’s because we think we already know the answers. Other times, it’s because we’re too proud to be perceived as not knowing. But is it so bad to want to know more about something as important as what we say we believe? Let’s learn to ask with the heart of a seeker instead of a cynic. Yes, God already knows what He’s going to do. Yet He invites us to take part in it anyway. Mon Reyes 

### Prayer

Jesus, I thank You for Your goodness. Thank You that You choose to reveal Yourself to me. Help me know You more and more each day. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Louis of France

## 2026-08-27 — How Much Time

### Reflection

“Therefore keep watch, because you do not know on what day your Lord will come. – Nine days before her 48th birthday, my sister who was just diagnosed with cancer, underwent a medical procedure. Hundreds of people prayed with us for her operation. We were in high spirits and we even took a selfie before she went in the operating room. It never once crossed my mind that the procedure would fail. Just hours later, she died in the ICU. God had other plans. If there is one good thing from this experience, it’s the reminder that life is a gift. It’s an opportunity to love and appreciate those around us. Every day is a chance to apologize to the people we have hurt and to forgive those who have hurt us. It’s an opportunity to be kinder, more loving, and more caring. I wish I had more time with my sister, but I’m grateful for the time we had, no matter how limited. Friends, the fact that you’re reading this today means you are blessed with the ultimate resource—time. Let’s not waste it with pettiness and bitterness. After all, we never know how much time we really have left. Ped Faytaren 

### Prayer

Jesus, I don’t know what tomorrow brings so help me live my best life today. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Monica

## 2026-08-28 — Are You Ready?

### Reflection

“So you, too, must keep watch! For you do not 1 know the day or hour of my return.” Last year, we lost a great friend, colleague, brother, and leader in the community, Tito Bimbo Leonardo. It was a sad yet glorious moment for his family, friends, and community. Why glorious, you may ask? In my eulogy, I shared three things that I fondly remember him by. First, Tito Bimbo showed and made me aware of what being loved and accepted is all about. Second, he was a wise man who took the liberty of making sure that he fulfilled what he needed to do. Third, which I feel made a great impact, he showed me that even if he was afraid, he was ready to be with God. The last words he uttered that I personally witnessed were, “See you when I get there.” It was a powerful statement that showed peace and readiness. This attitude could only come from a life well lived, made ready by a strong relationship he built with Tito Bimbo reminded me to always keep watch, deepen my faith, and be alert when God intervenes. Mike Ybiernas 

### Prayer

Lord, give me a peaceful heart to always be ready for Your return. St. Augustine of Hippo, bishop and Doctor of the Church,

## 2026-08-30 — Duped?

### Reflection

“You duped me, O Lord, and I let myself be duped.” – “Lord, why did I ever meet You? Now I face so many trials.” That was of my rare and candid questions when I was confronted by life’s struggles. As a Feast builder, husband, and father of two, I carry responsibilities that sometimes feel too heavy—leading a ministry, providing for my family, and securing our finances. “Lord, why is this so hard?” Jeremiah’s words in our key verse resonate deeply. I, too, have struggled with doubts, wondering if I am on the right path. Yet, just as Jeremiah couldn’t hold back proclaiming God’s Word, I, too, cannot ignore the fire in my heart. Even when I want to quit, something within me pushes forward. Surrender is the only way. When I let go of my plans and trust in His, things begin to unfold wonderfully. Obedience is hard, but God’s faithfulness is matchless. He will provide— and oftentimes, His provision is His presence by our side. For me, that is always more than enough. Stephen Nellas 

### Prayer

Jesus, my Savior, help me to be brutally honest with You, knowing that You will never abandon me even when I’m full of doubt. St. Jeanne Jugan,

St. Jeanne Jugan

## 2026-08-31 — Good News Of Healing

### Reflection

The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because 1 He has chosen me to bring good news to the poor . . . recovery of sight to the blind, to set 100, 101, 102 free the oppressed. – GNB Lorna had talent, capabilities, and determination. Her company was flourishing and would soon be recognized as number one in the industry. After 10 years, she was reaping the laurels, fame, and money. She didn’t believe in God. One day, after an annual health checkup, her doctor found a tumor in her brain. She brushed it off. But as months went by, her headaches became worse, and she began to limp. She couldn’t work, and her friends came to console and pray for her, telling her about a God who can heal. But she brushed them off. The day came when she couldn’t walk anymore, and with a splitting headache, she cried out, “Lord! Are You there? Listen to me. I’m sorry. I know You love me and will heal me.” She shouted those words, crying out for help from the God she didn’t believe in. Today is Lorna’s second year of recovery. She can walk, and run, and the tumor is gone. She now believes in the God of love, who loved her then—and now too. Chelle S. Crisanto 

### Prayer

Lord, heal my broken body and spirit. You are my hope. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Raymond Nonnatus

## 2026-10-01 — More Than Comfort

### Reflection

“For the hand of God has struck me.” – I’m a father of two boys. I want a lot of things for them—to be healthy, intelligent, wealthy, and generous. But if there’s one important thing than all of those, it is for them to be godly. This is my priority. I want my kids to be loving, selfless, faithful—to be men who follow Now, think of God the Father. He wants to bless us abundantly and give us a wonderful life. But He prioritizes our character over our comfort because that’s what lasts forever. When we go through trials and difficulties, it’s so easy to complain, “God, why do You allow so many problems in my It’s possible that God allowed those difficulties so that our character, our faith, our trust, our endurance, and our love will be shaped and strengthened. He sees our suffering and meets us there. It’s not like He turns a blind eye on our pain. But there are times when He will allow us to go through trials so we become desperate for the more important nourishment that our soul needs—God Himself. Bo Sanchez

## 2026-10-03 — My Loving Inspiration

### Reflection

“Do not rejoice that the spirits submit to you, but rejoice that your names are written in heaven.” – There are a number of people who inspire me in my faith journey. Some are saints; others are my leaders. I also have amazing friends who bring me closer to Jesus. But in my eyes, there is one whose light shines the most. She has been serving God for more than two decades now. But more than her public ministry, I admire how she serves God privately. She always finds a way to give to someone in need, even a stranger. She empowers women and young people through her talks; she encourages her loved ones through her life. She’s a class valedictorian, college scholar, and well-loved author, yet she values her identity as a child of God more than all her successes and achievements. Her validation and peace do not come from her accomplishments but from her relationship with God. What a blessing to witness this! Maymay, my wife, is truly a loving inspiration to me. Watching her live out her faith reminds me that true rejoicing doesn’t happen when the world submits to us. We experience it when we submit to God’s love and see how He works in and through our lives. Ericson T. Trinidad 

### Prayer

Jesus, You are my joy. Give me the grace to submit to Your will. St. Mother Theodore Guérin,

## 2026-10-04 — Anxiety Reset

### Reflection

Have no anxiety at all, but in everything, by prayer and petition, with thanksgiving, make your requests known to God. – When I was still a deacon, I experienced the most horrific anxiety I stood beside the cardinal of Manila. We had been kneeling down for more than an hour as we participated in the procession of the Blessed Sacrament. The first half-hour was uneventful. But the next seemed to last forever. My stomach was churning; I was hyperventilating and was about to black out. Thankfully, nothing dramatic happened. My counselor gave me tips on how to survive attacks like that. Then my spiritual director invited me to look deeper into the causes. Later on, I discovered I had a lot of anxiety caused by false expectations and pressures that I placed upon myself. I had to remind myself, especially as a deacon and eventually as a priest, that I am not God. For me to yield fruit for the Kingdom, I need to place my trust in Jesus, who already suffered completely for me because of His love and mercy. Fr. Migs Ramirez 

### Prayer

Jesus already gave His life for you. What is still making you anxious? Father, thank You for sending Your Son, Jesus. Today, I renew my trust in Him and offer to You all my worries and fears. Amen.

St. Francis of Assisi

## 2026-10-05 — Grace To Love

### Reflection

“And you shall love your neighbor as yourself.” – To love another as I love myself. Such weighty words these are. And such a taller order to do. Yet all God ever asks of us is summed up in this. The Gospel says: Love God and love others as you love yourself. It’s the second greatest commandment. This is the very core of our faith, emphasizing the call to walk in another’s shoes, acting with kindness and embracing Imagine that person who irks you—the one who darkens the room for you when she walks in. The one who stops you on your tracks because he is walking toward you. Can you love him? Can you spend time and listen to her? Honestly, it’s a struggle for me. That’s why I need grace. Lallaine Gogna

## 2026-10-06 — Changed

### Reflection

They only heard the report: “The man who formerly persecuted us is now preaching the faith he once tried to destroy.” And they 14-15 praised God because of me. – I know a man who is an ex- convict. He came into our center for the homeless with a lot of pride. He acted like he knew it all and didn’t need guidance from anyone. He signed up but said our formation program was not for him—that there was nothing more to be molded or formed in him. But the daily immersion in prayer, God’s Word, and the kindness and compassion of formators and staff changed him. He began to treat his enemies with love. And while the years on the streets taught him to stay tough and only look out for his own interests, he allowed God’s Word to direct his path. Today, he serves as an inspiration in our center as one who has turned away from sin and selfishness. Just like Saint Paul in our First Reading, he speaks freely of his life’s ongoing transformation through God’s grace. While this man seeks to restore self-worth by gaining proper employment, he has already reclaimed his identity as God’s beloved child. Geraldine G. Catral

“Jesus, teach us to pray, just as John taught his disciples.” – (or ) (or Today’s Gospel speaks deeply to 52-53, 54-55) me. The disciples could have figured out a prayer on their own, but instead, they asked Jesus to teach them. And of the Rosary Jesus, in His love, showed them how. ## 2026-10-08 — Good Gifts

### Reflection

“If you then . . . know how to give good gifts to your children, how much more will the Father in heaven give the holy Spirit to those 72, 73-75 who ask him?”– I love giving gifts to my children. But if they ask for bad things, say prohibited drugs, I won’t definitely It’s the same with our Father in heaven. “Ask and you shall receive.” We’re familiar with this verse, which we read in the Gospel today. Yet, many of us miss and understand the verses “If you then. . . know how to give good gifts . . .” Don’t be misled into thinking that anything and everything you ask for, you shall receive, because Jesus clearly qualifies that they should be “good gifts.” We may think that what we’re asking for are good for us—like more money, which isn’t intrinsically bad. Yet, if God knows that it will only harm you, He either won’t give it, or delay it until you’re ready, or replace it with something better. So go ahead, ask from the Father in heaven, and you will receive them. If they are “good gifts” as He sees them, you’ll surely receive! Alvin Barcelona 

### Prayer

Dear God, grant me the grace to ask of You and to surrender to Your will if what I ask is truly good for me. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Benedicta

## 2026-10-10 — Without Conditions

### Reflection

For through faith you are all children of God in Christ Jesus. – For a long time, my prayers and service were transactional. My prayers were filled with bargains and pleas, always promising one thing in return for something else. One time during worship, I was bargaining with the Lord yet again. “Father, what do You want me to do in exchange for this?” I began listing down my offers, promising to do this and that in exchange for an answered prayer. It was in the middle of my tearful worship when God broke through my pleas and said, “My child, you don’t have to do a thing. You are My daughter. I love you. There are no conditions. Now come and tell Me what it is that you want.” What a foreign concept it was to be loved without conditions! But that’s what God does. He loves us, chose us, and died for us, without us having to do a thing. We’ve already been redeemed and restored into God’s embrace through Jesus’ blood. We only have to receive it and believe. Karren Renz P. Seña

## 2026-10-11 — I Almost Gave Up

### Reflection

I can do all things through him who strengthens me. – NRSV A few months ago, I took the IELTS exam, aiming only for the (or )band scores required by my chosen Australian university. Though I teach writing, I feared the writing test, as I had friends who scored 6.0 and retook it many times. Since I was on a tight budget, I prayed I wouldn’t have to retake any skill. On test day, just as my writing test started, I urgently needed to go to the toilet. Since the restroom was outside the center, I had to run, losing 10 precious minutes from my test time. With only 50 minutes left for both writing tasks, I rushed to finish it, even if it meant getting a low score. Then I remembered that paying for a retake was costly. I took a deep breath and prayed a Hail Mary, focusing on every word. I remembered the verse above and held on to it as I completed both tasks just in time. When I received my results, I was overjoyed—I had scored higher than I expected that the center even invited me for an interview. Truly, with God, all things are possible. Elaine Marie Factor

Liberation: Christ liberates us from sin’s weight. ## 2026-10-13 — What The World Needs

### Reflection

When you’re joined to the Anointed One, circumcision and religious obligations can benefit you nothing. All that matters now 44, 45, 47, 48 is living in the faith that works and expresses itself through love. – TPT I have a four-year-old daughter who loves to dress up. She’d ask, “Do I look pretty, Mommy? Do you like my dress?” As a proud momma, I would affirm her choices, even though sometimes she would choose a colorful floral dress matched with fruit- printed leggings. But I’d also remind her that what truly makes a person beautiful—inside and out—is their kindness, no matter how nicely or simply they’re dressed. Today’s reading reminds us that while the sacraments and our daily devotions are there to enrich our faith and guide our life, our mission goes beyond living a prayerful life. We are called to live out our faith through genuine, active love— through daily acts of kindness and charity, by spreading joy and peace, by sharing time and resources, by showing mercy and forgiveness, and by serving others. ## 2026-10-14 — Loved By A Just God

### Reflection

“For you are careful to tithe even the tiniest income from your herb gardens, but you ignore justice and the love of God.” The last subject I took in my master’s degree is Social Justice. Every day, we see a lot of injustice in the world. So the entire time, our professor discussed social issues about injustice. Is God a just God? How do we have God’s justice in a world full of injustices? Receiving God’s justice is a concept that can be both comforting and challenging. It often brings to mind God’s righteous nature—His perfect ability to judge fairly, to uphold what is right, and to restore balance in the world. For many of us believers, God’s justice means knowing that, ultimately, He will right the wrongs in the world—whether that’s in our personal lives, in society, or on a global scale. However, receiving God’s justice can also be humbling. It reminds us that while His justice is always fair, His grace allows us to be redeemed and reconciled with Him, even though we fall short of His perfection. In the end, God’s justice is His unconditional love for us. Joel N. Saludares

Lord, in Your unconditional love, we always find complete justice. Saint Callistus I, pope and martyr,

## 2026-10-15 — Pretentious Talker

### Reflection

“Woe to you experts in the law, because you have taken away the key to knowledge. You yourselves have not entered, and you have hindered those who were entering.” – One day, a young boy was caught stealing his classmate’s bond paper. His teacher told him to bring his parents to the principal’s office the following day. With disappointment and frustration in his voice, the father told his son, “Haven’t I told you that it’s bad to take stuff that’s not yours? Is not the ream of bond paper I brought home from my boss’s office not enough for you?” Jesus loves and reaches out to the greatest of sinners. But one thing He hates most is the hypocrisy of the Pharisees and religious leaders of His time. He has no problem with their religious know-how but how strict they were about the law while being the first violators of it. They talked about holiness and righteousness, but they didn’t observe them. In a world filled with many opinions on social media, we don’t need more talkers but doers. People are not mesmerized by perfection but are moved by authenticity. People can easily spot if your words don’t match your actions. May we be effective witnesses of God’s love and goodness. Velden Lim 

### Prayer

Lord, I know I’m not perfect, but may my imperfect struggle to align my actions to Your Word inspire others to draw closer to You. St. Teresa of Avila, virgin,

## 2026-10-18 — Faith, Love, And Hope

We remember before our God and Father your work produced by faith, your labor prompted by love, and your endurance 9-10 inspired by hope in our Lord Jesus Christ. Paul mentioned three important Christian virtues in our key verse: faith, love, and hope, which we need to be reminded of always. Faith—for believing that God is always with us. No matter what we go through in life, even if we don’t see, hear, or feel Him, He is actually with us. Love—that we should always act with charity, even when it’s not convenient, because God loved us first. And hope—that no matter what situation we’re in, God always has the last say in our lives. It may not be how we pictured or dreamed it to be, but God’s plan for our lives will always prevail if we allow Him to mold and shape us every day. Faith, love, and hope—these are three Christian virtues we need to always return to when we experience the turbulent waves of life. Nico Narvaez

## 2026-10-19

### Reflection

“Teacher, tell my brother to share the inheritance with me.” – Siblings fight over the littlest of things when we were young—who gets the chicken drumstick, who reads the comics first, who has more minutes on the bike, who gets more beans in the halo-halo. We never graduate from greed. The only thing that changes is what we fight about when we get older: properties and inheritance. Family reunions that used to happen in the living room now happen in the courtroom. What brings back peace in the family? “Pattern interrupt.” You interrupt the pattern of greed and short-circuit the system of envy among siblings. Don’t insist on fairness and equality, or the bickering won’t stop. Someone makes the sacrifice. Someone agrees to take the smaller share while the rest get more. When you do this, everyone falls silent. You become the genuine leader of the family. You get the authority to speak and bring back love. Next, God finds a way to reward you. And this reward is better than what everyone is fighting about. Why fight over that little slice of pizza when God is giving you a pizza factory? Friend, peace in the family has a price. It’s sacrifice. Are you paying it? Jon Escoto 

### Prayer

Jesus, give me the courage to lead and the love to make the sacrifice so that my family can heal. Amen.

Saint Isaac Jogues and companions

## 2026-10-23 — Improve Our Gini

### Reflection

Make every effort to keep the unity of the Spirit through the bond of peace. – Gini coefficient is an index that measures the degree of inequality in the distribution of wealth in a country. The higher the number, the higher the inequality. Scandinavian countries enjoy low Gini coefficients in the 0.20s. The Philippines is around 0.45, while South Africa tops it at 0.60s. When the gap between rich and poor becomes too wide, it creates injustice and disunity. No matter how united we are in faith, ideologies, and family values, we cannot be unified when people on top live in excess while the rest can hardly put food on the table. We can’t totally eradicate poverty, but we can help each other achieve financial peace. Numerous studies show that the way to be financially healthy is to automate saving and investing. No amount of financial education can match the positive effect of even just a minor design tweak in the salary structure incorporating this automaticity. If I could ask a genie for a wish, I’d ask for automatic saving and investing for everyone to improve our gini coefficient! But God is no genie. He teaches us to make the effort. And by His grace, we shall! Rose Fres Fausto

## 2026-10-25 — On Love And Rivals

### Reflection

And he said to him, “You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, and with all your soul, and with all your mind.” I grew up with constant worry about money, shelter, security, and what the future holds. I brought all this baggage to adulthood, which developed into anxiety. But what is anxiety if not anticipating the worst, while underestimating my ability to cope? My default was to be prepared, be on guard, and be anxious. Counseling, trusted friends, and my community at The Feast, a weekly prayer gathering, helped me a lot to be mindful whenever I get overwhelmed. But from time to time, I still catch myself spiraling with my thoughts. It’s like my heart is pulled in different directions where there is always something to worry about. I’d say God’s biggest rival in my heart is anxiety. I’m still learning day by day how to let earthly matters find their place around God—and not the other way around. So when failure, sickness, loneliness, and even death come, my heart will be broken, but it will not be divided. Because it belongs to God alone. Rinabelle Cabagyo

## 2026-10-31 — Victory In Humility

I assumed he explained the situation because the guard walked back to his post afterwards. That was a profound defining moment for me. ## 2026-11-01 — You’Re A Giant

After a few minutes, I realized my wallet had vanished. I was devastated. I couldn’t sleep that night! Because my wallet had P500, equal to a million pesos for a poor missionary like me! Fast forward 30 years later. I was buying bottled water. I dug through my pocket for my P500. But it probably fell. So I got my wallet and paid for the water. Yes, I still felt a loss, but I slept soundly that night. Why? Because I’m now a millionaire. The only reason you give in to bitterness and pettiness is because you think you’re small. ## 2026-11-02 — Remember The

### Reflection

2 Maccabees 12:43-46 For if he were not expecting the fallen to rise (or Wisdom 3:1-9) again, it would have been superfluous and foolish to pray for the dead. 15-16, 17-18 (or – 2 Maccabees 12:44 (or ) I always hear it at Mass and talks given by priests. I read about it in books and even in the Bible. But I didn’t really take to heart the importance of praying for the dead. I used to think that people who passed on already had their chance in life. We prayed for them and offered Mass during their wake and that’s it. But what about those who were not able to hear the Word of God proclaimed to them? What about those who heard but did not understand fully? Or what about those who reject Jesus because they didn’t understand what they were doing? Will they be damned to hell? It didn’t make sense to me. As the Church continues its mission, She doesn’t forget those who’ve gone before us. They’re included in our prayers. So I started to do that—to pray for them daily and ask God’s mercy to accept them into His Kingdom. Edwin Marcelo

## 2026-11-03 — The Privilege Of

### Reflection

“Blessed is the one who will dine in the kingdom of God.” – “Nope. I just opened a business. I got my hands full,” said Jake who was being invited to serve in the worship team. “Negative. I just bought my dream bike and I’ll be riding with my friends on weekends,” said Jack who was being tapped to lead a men’s support group. “Can’t. I just got married. We’re busy with the transition,” said Jade who was being asked to help out in the media team. Very valid reasons. The people invited in the Gospel turned down the invitation because of work, recreation, and family. But I believe the Lord will not ask us to neglect and sacrifice our jobs, rest, and families at the altar of service. These things are also a calling that we must steward responsibly. But today’s Gospel is a sobering reminder that God’s invitation to serve is not just a duty but a privilege. Why are we saying no to His call? What outweighs the invitation of God in our lives? Let’s keep our priorities in order, while keeping God at the top. He has prepared a banquet for us. Blessed is the one who will eat at the feast in His Kingdom. Veia Lim-Viñas (@veialimvinas)

### Prayer

Lord, forgive me for the times I’ve put other things above You. Help me see service not as a burden but as a privilege. Give me a willing heart and the wisdom to balance my responsibilities. Amen.

## 2026-11-04 — Hardcore

### Reflection

“If anyone comes to me and does not hate . . . even their own life—such a person cannot be my disciple. And whoever does not carry their cross and follow me cannot be my disciple.” I have a friend from the singles ministry that society may call a “hardcore Catholic.” She goes to confession almost every week, wears a veil during Mass, fasts even when it’s not Lent, and has a collection of religious items. There’s a part of me that thinks, “Isn’t that a bit much, Lord?” But you’d do all those for the one you love, right? So I asked, “Is what I do enough and pleasing to the Lord?” After all, He has shown me the greatest kind of love by laying down His life. What Jesus did for me can never compare to wearing a veil in church or going to Mass and confession often. I am reminded that I can never have too much of Jesus because He gave His entirety to me. So I hope to reciprocate it in my own little way. Thankfully, Jesus doesn’t ask for much. Just our open hearts. May every day be a reminder for us to be unashamedly in love with Jesus! Jewelle Q. Villarisco 

### Prayer

Jesus, thank You for not holding Yourself back from fully loving me. Help me do the same for You and my neighbors. Amen.

## 2026-11-05 — You Are Counted

### Reflection

“If a man has a hundred sheep and one of them gets lost, what will he do? Won’t he leave the ninety-nine others in the wilderness and go to search for the one that is lost until he finds it?” – When we look at a crowd, that’s all we tend to see—a crowd. We see people, not individuals. Just a mass of faces, like a It’s the same with sheep. When you look at a hundred of them, you don’t see one. You see a blur of wool, all alike. None But not so with the shepherd. A good shepherd knows each of his sheep. He sees their faces. He knows their stories. That’s the point of the parable Jesus told. When the shepherd goes after the one that’s lost, it’s because every sheep matters. Each one is unique. Each one is known. Each one is And that includes you. Believe this, my friend: you matter to God. Even in a crowd, He sees you. Your story matters to Him. You are never just one of many. You are counted. Arun Goggna 

### Prayer

God seeks you out because you matter to Him. Thank You, Lord, for letting me know that I am counted. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Elizabeth

## 2026-11-06 — The Right Use

### Reflection

“Prepare a full account of your stewardship.” – “Bro, park in that slot on your next visit. It’s free and secure too,” my friend Don said. He regularly invited me to preach to his prayer group called the Edsa Shrine Charismatic Community. Finding a free slot there took time because it was a busy mall. Then I’d rush to the church to give my talk. That’s why his offer was great news for me. “The slot belongs to the church, but they gave me full authority to decide who can use it during our prayer meeting days,” he said. His reply reminded me of what stewardship is all about. I knew Don as a great steward of everything that God gave him. Sadly, I won’t see Don anymore because he unexpectedly died a month after our last conversation. Today’s Gospel reminds us that we should use all our possessions wisely. We need to remember that everything we have belongs to God. We are merely stewards of it so we should use them for His glory. Alvin Fabella 

### Prayer

Lord, teach me to be wise with everything You gave. Allow me to appreciate that eternal rewards are greater than any earthly possessions I have. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Joseph Khang

## 2026-11-07 — What God Entrusted

### Reflection

“Whoever can be trusted with very little can also be trusted with much, and whoever is dishonest with very little will also be dishonest with much.” – God has given each of us responsibilities. Some are big, some are small. I think about the roles He has entrusted to me—a wife, a mother, a worker, a friend. Sometimes, these responsibilities feel overwhelming. The weight of daily tasks, caring for others, and trying to do my best can be exhausting. But how do I carry these responsibilities? Do I do them with joy or do I complain? Do I see them as blessings or do I let bitterness creep in? It’s easy to ask God for more—more success, more opportunities, more blessings. But am I truly ready for more? God gives more to those He trusts can handle it. If He calls me to something greater, it means He believes in me. He knows my heart, my strengths, and my limits better than anyone. Instead of feeling burdened, let’s choose gratitude. Let’s trust that God has given us exactly what we need. And in His time, He will give us more. Mia Bueno 

### Prayer

Lord, help me embrace my responsibilities with love. Strengthen me to serve with joy and trust in Your perfect plan. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Engelbert

## 2026-11-08 — The Dash

### Reflection

“Therefore, stay awake, for you know neither Wisdom 6:12-16 the day nor the hour.” – (or 1 Thessalonians Last week, we celebrated All Saints and All Souls Days, so the verse above is very timely. Nobody truly knows when God will call us home. That’s why we should always be ready. Life is short—as short as a dash—that little line between the date of birth and the date of death that will be written on our tombstones. Let me share some lines from the poem “The Dash” by For it matters not how much we own, the cars, the house, the cash. What matters is how we live and love, and how we spend our dash. . . . If we treat each other with respect and more often wear a smile, remembering that this special dash might only last a little while. So, when your eulogy is being read with your life’s actions to rehash, would you be proud of the things they say about how you spent your dash? Let’s live ready to meet the Lord anytime! Alvin Barcelona

### Prayer

Dear God, thank You for my life. May I live it with love for You and for others, so that when my time comes, I will continue loving You and being loved by You forever. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Castorius

## 2026-11-09 — Zeal For God

### Reflection

His disciples remembered that it is written: “Zeal for your house will consume me.” – One advantage of homeschooling is that you get to take charge of your of the Lateran Basilica child’s education. It’s also challenging for the same reason, especially when it comes to matters of faith. I grew up serving in church. I joined religious orgs. I received Catechism in school. I even taught Christian Living as a substitute teacher. But admittedly, there are many things about our faith that I’ve forgotten or don’t know. So how do I help my kids grow up Catholic and develop zeal for God? Thankfully, there are tools both online and offline about God. For long car rides, there are podcasts about saints or Scripture. There are a variety of books about the sacraments, saints, and Catholic teachings. What’s beautiful is we learn together. I see again with new eyes what they discover for the first time. Their interest encourages me! I try to model spirituality, even if I struggle with faith at times. I trust the Lord to fill the gaps in my efforts. He will give my children a heart that seeks Him and is filled with zeal for His house. Kitty Dulay-Ferreria 

### Prayer

Lord, bring my faith to life so that I can influence others for You. Saint Benignus,

### Saint of the Day

Saint Benignus

## 2026-11-10 — The Great Reward

### Reflection

“So should it be with you. When you have done all you have been commanded, say, ‘We are unprofitable servants; we have done what 27, 29 we were obliged to do.’” – “What’s in it for me?” When you do something good for others, do you expect a reward? Would you be kind, generous, and compassionate even if you didn’t receive recognition or praise in return? It all boils down to our motives. Who are we pleasing? Why do good at all when we think it won’t have an immediate impact on the world around us or that we get nothing in return? Worse, we can even get rejected! Living a courageous, faithful, holy, and loving life according to God’s will is not about what we get in return. It’s who we are becoming because we were created not just good, but very good. So if you feel your hard work hasn’t paid of or if you’re more focused on the externals, remember that it’s the internal and long-lasting growth that is life-giving. In the end, this world can’t give the real reward we seek. For it is God Himself who is our great reward. Tony P. Valenzuela 

### Prayer

Lord, help me to see the good in myself and in others. Help me overcome the need to be rewarded by this world and instead seek You always, my great and true reward. Amen.

## 2026-11-11 — Resisting Happiness

### Reflection

For we ourselves were once foolish, disobedient, led astray, slaves to various passions and pleasures . . . He saved us, not because of works done by us in righteousness, but according to his own mercy. – One of my favorite prayers at the start of the day is, “Holy Spirit, direct my steps.” This gives me peace to let go of control and allow the Lord to lead my day. I found that it’s so much more fulfilling that way. Once upon a time, I was the lord of my life. But I realized that I was only leading myself to a life of confusion, emptiness, I thank God for the sacrament of confession that allows the Holy Spirit to work in and through me powerfully again. From a podcast, I learned that the meaning of holy is to be set apart, to be unique, to look different from the rest of the world. The devil tempts us through worldly pleasures because he wants us to be enslaved, while the Lord wants to set us free to be who He created us to be. I read a book titled Resisting Happiness whose main message said: when we are resisting holiness, we are resisting I found this to be true, again and again. Marianne Mencias 

### Prayer

Jesus, may I always say yes to holy joy, for it is what I am made for. St. Martin of Tours, bishop,
