## 2026-11-12

### Reflection

I appeal to you for my son Onesimus.” – Have you ever received a request from a friend appealing on someone’s behalf to forgive them for a wrong they’ve done? Maybe you’re a mother asking your son to forgive his older brother or a manager requesting a second chance for an erring employee. Philemon was a Christian in Colossi who received such a request from Paul. Philemon’s slave, Onesimus, fled to Rome after stealing from him. Onesimus became a Christian after encountering Paul who was in prison. Paul wrote a letter to Philemon, pleading with him to accept Onesimus back, not as a slave but as a beloved brother in Christ. Paul’s willingness to pay for Onesimus’s debt reminds us of Christ paying for our sins. It’s a beautiful true story of a peacemaker mediating between two opposing parties and facilitating reconciliation. Are you called to do the same today? Perhaps a sincerely written letter can also move the aggrieved party to give mercy. Marjorie Duterte 

### Prayer

God.” (Matthew 5:9) Lord, make me an instrument of Your peace. Where there is hatred, let me bring Your love. Where there is injury, pardon. (Prayer of Saint Francis) Saint Josaphat, bishop and martyr,

## 2026-11-13 — A Walk To Remember

### Reflection

It has given me great joy to find some of your 2 children walking in the truth. – 2 NIV During my scheduled one- on-one with Bro. Bo Sanchez, he introduced me to a walking meeting. Instead of having food or coffee on a table, we walked around Greenbelt in Makati. From one floor to another, we shared insights, mentorship points, and stories. It reminded me of other memorable walks I had in my life. Walking to the wet market with my mom when I was small. The long mall walk with my girlfriend, now my wife, while holding her hand. Walking while carrying my two kids. And the unforgettable pilgrimage from Las Piñas to Luneta for World Youth Day in 1995. We walk in all areas of our lives. Our walk toward health, wealth, relationships, self mastery, dreams­—every destination is a journey. And the best walk is the one we do with God. So don’t walk alone. Walk with people God sends to your life to make the journey bearable and meaningful. Walk with God today. Dreus Cosio 

### Prayer

Dear Lord, our journey in this life is both challenging and amazing. But walking with You is the best thing we can do. Thank You for sending people who help us in our journey. When we get tired, please carry us. Amen.

## 2026-11-14 — ‘Immaculate’ Widow

### Reflection

“Because this widow keeps pestering me, 3 I will see to it that she gets justice.” Immaculate. That word describes me perfectly. . . when pronounced this way: /I’m-ma-kulit/. Makulit, Filipino for persistent, stubborn, pesky even. Jesus must’ve had me in mind when He told the Parable of the Persistent Widow. She’s me to a T when I pray. Some of my daily petitions are answered within reasonable time, while others are already celebrating milestone birthdays. But even if those prayers remain unanswered for years, I continue to pester God for them. As Fr. Dave Concepcion says, “Ask. How will God grant your prayers if you don’t ask?” And I don’t just ask. I pester. I remind God about His promises. “Ask and it will be given to you” (). “If you ask anything of Me in My name, I will do it” (). “Whatever you ask the Father in My name He will give You” And I never fail to remind Him He is “a Father to the fatherless, a defender of widows” (). I know that in His perfect time I’ll receive the best versions of my prayers. Lella M. Santiago 

### Prayer

God is not deaf to the deep desires you have been praying for. Keep praying. God’s answer may still be a work in progress. Lord, when my spirit flags, help me to pray without ceasing. Saint Clementinus,

### Saint of the Day

Saint Clementinus

## 2026-11-15 — What If God Doesnt

### Reflection

“To one he gave five talents; to another, two; 30-31 to a third, one—to each according to his ability.” – ‭‭Matthew‬ ‭25‬:‭15 1 I would read a lot of stories that (or )mention how God answered their prayers. They use it as evidence of how much God loves them or how much they felt His love when He finally granted their prayers. But what if God doesn’t grant what you asked for? Does it mean that He isn’t crazy in love with you? When my son was sick, I was afraid of how I would feel if God didn’t answer my prayers for him. But long ago, I’ve already made up my mind that God is Healer no matter what So when my son passed away, I experienced a tsunami of God’s love that just enveloped me amidst the sadness. The graces and consolation I received surpassed the graces that would have been mine if he was healed. Definitely, God provides us with more than what we will ever need. Ronna Singson Ledesma

God always gives us what we need, not what we want. It’s up to us to multiply it. Abba Father, may I never waste any grace that You give me. St. Albert the Great, bishop and Doctor of the Church,

## 2026-11-16 — Jesus Is Passing By

### Reflection

“Jesus . . . have mercy on me . . . I want to see!” ; 2:1-5 – NLT My eye bulged out of proportion when the area around my left eye became swollen. I had to be in the hospital for repeated tests and antibiotics. The doctors couldn’t determine the cause of the swelling. It was painful, itchy at times, and my sight was slowly blurring. I don’t want to be blind! It was during the triduum of Holy Week that, miraculously, the swelling subsided and my eye eventually returned to normal. That challenge gave me time to reflect and surrender to the Lord much more, trusting in His Will. I got to value more what I’d ordinarily take for granted. It made me “see” things in a better light. It taught me patience, perseverance and trust. Moreso, I was so grateful for the mercy the Lord showed me in restoring my sight and healing my eye. We don’t have to experience literal blindness for us to recognize when the Lord is “passing by.” Sometimes, it’s in our trials when we truly “see” Him. So don’t waste any challenge, suffering, or sickness. Listen to Him and grow in your relationship with Him. Have faith. Let Him reveal to you the miracles waiting for you to see. J Yogawin 

### Prayer

Jesus, please increase my faith. I want to see Your will for me. St. Margaret of Scotland,

### Saint of the Day

St. Margaret of Scotland

## 2026-11-17 — The Action Of Grace

### Reflection

“Zacchaeus, come down quickly, for today I must stay at your house.” – NAB I love our Gospel story because Zacchaeus and I are both “short in stature.” Jesus shows us three beautiful Jesus stops. “Jesus . . . intended to pass through the town . . . Jesus, who was about to pass that way. When He reached the place, Jesus looked up and said to him, ‘Zacchaeus . . .’” (verses 1-5). For Jesus, every person is worth stopping for. Let’s ask ourselves: Do we stop for people or do we pass them by because we have more important things to do? Jesus sees. “When He reached the place, Jesus looked up and said to him, ‘Zacchaeus, come down quickly . . .’” (verse 5). Jesus’ gaze conveyed His transforming grace. Let’s ask ourselves: Do we take notice of people who need our love and service? Jesus stays. “Jesus looked up and said to him, ‘Zacchaeus, come down quickly, for today I must stay at your house.’” Despite His whirlwind mission schedule, Jesus stayed and gave quality time to people He cared about. Let’s ask ourselves: whom do we need to stay with and give quality time to? Obet Cabrillas 

### Prayer

Lord, may I imitate Your brand of love such that I stop, see, and stay with the people You love. Let me be a channel of Your grace, in Jesus’ name. Amen.

## 2026-11-18 — Sweat The Small Stuff

### Reflection

He replied, “Well done, good servant! You have been faithful in this very small matter; take charge of ten cities.” – “If you measure it, you can Dedication of the Basilicas change it.” This is one of the things of Saints Peter and Paul, I learned when I decided to keep a healthy lifestyle. This was difficult because I disliked routine, repetition, and rules while I loved being unique, deviant, and sometimes shocking! I would start a habit, only to break it after three days. But I realized that I wasn’t getting any younger and being always “unpredictable” Thankfully, friends held me accountable to my overall health. Using a simple tracking sheet, I learned how to write down daily stats like steps, calorie and protein intake, goals and habits, even the highs and lows of my day. By measuring these small steps, I saw how each of them contributed to an overall feeling of happiness, clarity, and success. Bonus, I lost 10 kilograms in the process! Sometimes, we always expect big things to happen in our lives. But we forget that these big things are only results of the small, measurable habits that we do. Fr. Migs Ramirez 

### Prayer

God wants to give you only the best. But are you doing your part in being faithful in the small virtuous habits each day? Father, in the daily grind of life and in the small and seemingly insignificant moments, may I always choose to be Your instrument of love. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Anselm

## 2026-11-19 — Born Pinoy!

### Reflection

As he drew near, he saw the city and wept over it. – One of my biggest errors in reading the Bible is that my image of my heroes are framed within a purely spiritual backdrop. I forget that Moses, Joshua, Nehemiah, Jeremiah, Joseph, son of Jacob, and others are lovers of their own people, Israel. They are fiercely loyal to the people that God called them to serve. Their holiness is expressed in their patriotism. Their love for God and for country are inseparable. Jesus wept for His city. When was the last time you wept for your country? No, I don’t advocate pure sentimentalism. There are two things we can do: One, hope in the Filipino. We are a young country. “Discovered” in 1521, we were just islands with tribes then. So don’t give up on this growing “kid.” We may be slow, but we are turning a corner. Instead of joining those who curse your country, spread this hope to others. Second, serve the Filipino. Someone said, “I love the Philippines. It’s the Filipinos I hate.” You don’t have to think of 116 million. Just feed one hungry person or teach a poor kid to read. Begin today. Jon Escoto 

### Prayer

Jesus, You made sure I was born a Filipino and You have a special mission for me. May I discover this mission and fulfill it. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Mechtildis

## 2026-11-20 — Sinking Sand

### Reflection

“‘My house shall be a house of prayer, but you have made it a den of thieves.’”– The Eyes of Tammy Faye is a 2021 film about the evangelist couple Tammy and Jim Bakker. In their show, The PTL Club, their prosperity Gospel promised heavenly blessings in exchange for donations. Cash and kind poured in In the 1980s, their Christian ministry operated a television network, hotel, water park, and residential complex. The famous couple displayed their beautiful houses and flashy cars. Eventually, investigations proved that membership funds were spent for their personal use. As more scandals surfaced, Tammy sang in tears, “On Christ the solid rock I stand/All other ground is sinking sand.” Jim spent five years in jail for fraud. The pastor turned God’s temple into a den, robbing followers by taking advantage of their trust. He may have started his ministry with a genuine call to serve, but his ego grew bigger than his fear of the Lord. When we pursue fame and material wealth, we build on sinking sand. Let’s keep God’s temple strong with sincere faith, for there is no other god before Him. Gina J. Verdolaga 

### Prayer

Lord, keep me humble before Your greatness, for You rule over heaven and earth. St. Edmund Rich,

St. Edmund Rich

## 2026-11-21 — For The Souls

### Reflection

Then they heard a loud voice from heaven (or ) saying to them, “Come up here.” During the pandemic, my heart (or ) ached for those who passed away of the Blessed Virgin Mary receiving the last sacraments before their last breath. So I was inspired to pray the rosary for them, offering it for one particular soul each day. Believing that praying for them will help in their purification and final entry to heaven, it has become part of my daily routine. I now have a tiny notebook where I have a list of deceased persons to pray for. ## 2026-11-22 — Death Doesn’T Get

### Reflection

The last enemy to be destroyed is death. 1 Sometimes, my mind spirals family member gets into an accident? What if a fire breaks out while we’re all asleep? What if one moment, life is normal, and the next, someone I love is gone? Movies and news stories make it feel all too real—how quickly everything can change, how fragile life is. These thoughts have gripped me with fear and often leave me restless and anxious. But Paul reminds us that Jesus’ resurrection changed everything. Death isn’t the end—it’s already been defeated. Jesus isn’t just alive—He reigns. And one day, He will put everything under His rule, including death itself. That means separation won’t last forever. Grief won’t have the final say. The people I love who belong to Jesus are never truly lost; they’re held safely in His hands, waiting in a place where sorrow The fear doesn’t disappear, but it loses its power. Because death doesn’t get the last word. Jesus does. And in Him, there is life that never ends. Tisha Alyssa Sanchez 

### Prayer

Lord, when I fear losing those I love, remind me that in You, death is not the end. Help me trust Your promise of eternal life. Amen.

Saint Cecilia

## 2026-11-23 — Treasures

### Reflection

He also saw a poor widow put in two very small copper coins. – NIV My room is filled with little treasures I’ve collected over the years—scrap paper, old water bottles with unique designs, stuffed animals, themed paper bags, even dried flowers. Some might call them “useless junk,” but if you ask me, I can tell you the story behind every single item and the memories they hold. My family often begs me to throw them out or donate them, but I always refuse. Today’s Gospel tells the story of a woman who reminds me of myself. She had only two small copper coins—something that, to the wealthy, seemed insignificant. But to her, it was everything. And Jesus praised her—because she gave from her poverty, not from her excess. Even though it may have hurt, she still chose to give. Jesus is asking us today to give from the places that hurt, where it’s uncomfortable, where it disrupts our ease. Because that’s when our giving truly means something. Helene Gogna 

### Prayer

Jesus, help me be like the widow—willing to give even from the little I have. Send me those who need Your loving help. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Columban

## 2026-11-24 — Break Down The Walls

### Reflection

“All that you see here—the days will come when there will not be left a stone upon another stone that will not be thrown down.” – When we’ve been hurt and betrayed, it’s easy to build a wall. We pile the bricks high until we believe we’re protected from anything that can wound us again. But when the wall is high enough, we realize we’ve boxed ourselves in. We’re stuck. We’re cornered by our fear and worries. We don’t know where to go, who to trust, and what to do. The only way out is a vast climb. We’re exhausted. Defeated. It’s another day when the enemy has won. Another day when doubt, insecurities, and shortcomings shroud us. We give up. But, a light shines through. When we thought all hope is lost, slowly, our walls crumble down. No stone remains unturned. Jesus reaches out, embracing us, because when we want to run and hide, He looks for us in spite of who we are and what we’ve done. Our God is merciful. Our God is faithful. Gemma Casimsiman 

### Prayer

Father, when I am lost and my strength is depleted, lead me back to You. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Andrew Dung-Lac

## 2026-11-25 — Rooted In Purpose

### Reflection

“But not a hair on your head will be destroyed. By your perseverance you will secure your lives.” – As I age, I sometimes feel as though I’m fading into the background, struggling to reclaim my place and my voice in a world that seems to have moved on without me. It’s as if aging is slowly eroding my worth, making me feel a little less seen, a little less heard. In these moments, the verse above reminds me that my worth is not defined by the world’s fleeting standards. It gives me the strength to balance the world’s view of me with my deeper, truer sense of worth. Even when I feel unnoticed, I’m comforted knowing that I’m always valued in God’s eyes. The life I’ve lived, the wisdom I’ve gained, and the love I’ve given are all part of His greater plan. Though I may no longer be at the center of things, I remain deeply rooted in God’s purpose through the relationships I’ve nurtured and the life I’ve led. I continue to walk in His will, standing firm in my roles as wife, mother, sister, mentor, and friend. I strive to leave a lasting legacy by living in a way that honors His eternal plan. Marie G. Ferrer 

### Prayer

Father, thank You for the purpose You’ve woven into every season of my life. As I face the challenges of aging, help me to continue to walk in Your will and fulfill my calling with grace and courage. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

St. Catherine of Alexandria

## 2026-11-26 — ‘I Shall Return’

### Reflection

“And then they will see the Son of Man ; coming in a cloud with power and great 19:1-3, 9 glory.” – Gen. Douglas MacArthur was the commander of the U.S. Army based in the Philippines during World War II. When it became imminent that the country would fall into the hands of the Japanese, President Roosevelt ordered MacArthur to evacuate to Australia. He and his troups left Corregidor in 1942 with a promise to the Filipino people, “I shall return.” Two years later, he landed in Leyte to fulfill that promise and liberated the Philippines from Japanese rule. Jesus also left us after He rose from the dead. During the ascension, His Apostles witnessed His bodily return to heaven. But He also promised, “I will come back and take you to be with Me” (). This is the mystery of our faith that we profess at Mass—that Jesus will come again. Yet many of us don’t understand that Christ’s Second Coming will be His bodily return to Earth to reign forevermore. When Jesus returns, He will liberate us from Satan’s evil forces and mankind will finally be free from sin and death. Rissa Singson Kawpeng 

### Prayer

Lord, let us live in a way that we can “stand erect and raise (our) heads because (our) redemption is at hand” (Luke 21:28). Saint Conrad,

Saint Conrad

## 2026-11-27 — Jesus Is Coming!

### Reflection

“In the same way, when you see –21:2 these things happening, know that the kingdom of God is near.” – I’ve met a lot of people who use the coming of Jesus as an excuse to be lazy. Many Christians say things like, “I don’t need to work hard; Jesus is about to come anyway.” Or, “If I get sick from my bad habits, it doesn’t matter; Jesus I’m not a theologian, but I believe there’s something wrong with that mindset. I don’t condemn them, but I believe we shouldn’t stop working on our lives just because Jesus is coming soon. In fact, we should work harder. We should use it as motivation instead of an excuse. We should say, “Work is tough, but I’ll work harder—Jesus is coming!” or “I want to take care of the body God gave me—Jesus is coming!” Jesus’ return should motivate us, not give us an excuse to quit. We don’t know when He will come, but our role is to tend the garden, our lives. We also need to love His people through acts of service and kindness. Nico Narvaez

## 2026-11-28 — Nothing Has To Change

### Reflection

“Be vigilant at all times and pray that you have the strength to escape the tribulations that are imminent and to stand before the Son of Man.” – In 1999, people were so afraid that the world was going to end at the turn of the millenium. There were doomsday preachers scaring people into repentance with stories of fire and brimstone. But the end of the world didn’t come. Again, people said that 2012 was the year because the Mayan calendar ended on 2012. So, well-meaning Christians preached repentance out of fear of the end times. But it didn’t I asked a wise friend what he thought about these things. I was shocked by what he said. “For others, maybe they need to get their act together. But we can’t control them anyway and so they shouldn’t be our worry. But would Jesus’ coming tomorrow change the way you serve today? Would it change the way you worship? The way you love? If the answer is no, then it doesn’t matter if Jesus returns tomorrow or in a million years. We’re living, loving, and serving as if Jesus is going to call us home any minute anyway. Nothing has to change.” Niko Capucion

## 2026-11-30

### Reflection

For it is with your heart that you believe and are justified, and it is with your mouth that you profess your faith. – Last year, we were blessed with an opportunity to visit Japan again. Our last day fell on a Sunday, and we attended Mass at a Catholic center near our hotel. We were amazed that most of the servants were Filipinos. What surprised me more was the Liturgy of the Word. It was the longest proclamation I heard, but for a good reason. The Mass was celebrated in English, but after each reading, the whole text was translated to Japanese. It was beautiful. But there were other incredible moments. Like when the priest came to us to bless Geriah, our baby. When a Filipina opened the elevator door so we could easily push our baby’s stroller inside. When a blind man played the organ during Mass. When two Filipinos offered to move our things to a more accessible area. When the attendees smiled at us empathetically when our son started running around during Mass. I realized then that no matter where we are across the globe, there are three universal languages that unite us all: faith, hope, and love. We profess them with our mouth and proclaim them with our lives. Ericson T. Trinidad

Feast of Saint Andrew,

## 2026-12-01 — When God Winks

### Reflection

“Blessed are the eyes that see what you see.” – I visited a friend on the day I was to fly to Dubai. I wanted to wear my thin, comfortable pants but couldn’t find it. I resigned myself to wearing the thick, stiff jeans I found in my closet. A dog barked ferociously inside the gate of my friend’s house. As I walked in, the dog bit my leg. Long story short, I had to go to the ER where a doctor said I needed rabies shots. I realized later that my thick jeans protected me from a more serious bite—and from missing my flight. Skeptics may say that’s just a coincidence, but as a person of faith, I recognized it as an action of God. When I arrived in Dubai, some attendees of The Feast, or Feasters as we call them, took me to a clinic for my second dose of injections. They shared their life stories with me as they kept me company. I was so inspired and thought to myself, I wouldn’t have heard these stories if that dog didn’t bite me. Friend, let’s train our eyes to recognize God and His movement in our lives. There are no coincidences—only “Godcidences.” Bo Sanchez 

### Prayer

God is always at work in you. You just need to open the eyes of your heart to see His hand orchestrating the events of your life. Open my eyes, Lord, that I may see You and hear You in the ordinary and extraordinary events of my life. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Blessed Charles de Foucauld

## 2026-12-02 — Faith In Action

### Reflection

“My heart is moved with pity for the crowd . . .” – Today’s Gospel shows us what drove Jesus to do miracles: compassion. He didn’t see the crowds as a difficulty to be overcome, but as people who need help. They brought their pain to Him—the blind, the lame, and the mute—and He healed them. The amazed reactions of people show how God’s power was made real through Jesus’ love. It’s important to remember that Jesus’ kindness goes beyond healing. He knows that they are hungry and vulnerable in that faraway place. He doesn’t want to send them away because He knows they’ll get hungry. He cares about all areas of their lives, taking care of their physical and Oh, how I wish we could all be like Jesus. May we be moved by compassion for our less fortunate brothers and sisters. As we believe that God will multiply our efforts and resources to help others, may we practice our Catholic faith and reflect God’s love and justice. Only then can we claim that we are living out our faith. Randy Borromeo

## 2026-12-03 — More Than Just Hearing

### Reflection

“Yet it did not fall, because it had its foundation on the rock.” – I read my Bible every day. I lead Bible studies. I go to church every Sunday. And I serve in different ministries. That should be enough, right? But following Jesus isn’t just about ticking all these It’s about not rolling my eyes when someone is testing my patience. It’s about forgiving people who never even said sorry—and probably never will. It’s holding back harsh words when I feel justified in speaking them. And honestly? That’s harder than reading my Bible. Jesus said it’s not enough to just call Him “Lord”—what matters is actually doing what He says. He compares those who listen but don’t obey to a house built on sand. It might look fine at first, but when the storm comes, it collapses. Following Jesus isn’t just about knowing His words. It’s about letting them sink in, take root within us, shape us, and change how we live. Not just on Sundays, not just when people are watching, but in the small, everyday choices that no one sees but Him. Tisha Alyssa Sanchez 

### Prayer

Lord, I don’t want to just hear Your words—I want to live by them. Help me build my life on Your truth so that when storms come, I will not be shaken. Amen.

## 2026-12-04 — Does Jesus Answer

### Reflection

“Because of your faith, it will happen.” Our Gospel today tells the story of two blind men whom Jesus healed. They followed Him and kept crying out for His mercy despite their condition. Just like those two blind men, I have been persistent in prayer and faith for 41 years, even when I don’t see immediate results. I have prayed as if everything depended on God and worked as if everything depended on me. This applies to praying for a house for our family, tuition fees for my children, healing when we are sick, working with my clients, and building The Feast Salcedo in Makati. Jesus asked, “Do you believe I am able to do this?” I doubt sometimes because I want Him to answer my prayers exactly the way I imagine. But faith doesn’t work like that. It requires true belief in God’s power and trust in His process. Today, I am reminded to trust God more. I have many unanswered prayers, but looking back at my life, He has already answered so many. He did it before; He can do it again. Jan Silan 

### Prayer

My Jesus, show me mercy like You did for the blind men. Help me to be persistent and to trust Your ways. Amen.

## 2026-12-05 — Forgive And Grow

### Reflection

“Freely you have received; freely give.” – –10:1, I was so competitive early in my corporate career. I often pushed for excellence, both in myself and others. This drive for success made forgiveness, especially towards myself, feel like a weakness. But my exposure to the right mentors and friends made me realize that mercy is not a sign of defeat but of strength and grace. Compassion and empathy are essential in allowing us to grow, as individuals and in our relationships. Being merciful isn’t about excusing flaws; it’s about recognizing the humanity in others and ourselves. It’s about understanding that all of us are imperfect and that mistakes are part of our journey. Let’s actively aspire to embrace self-compassion. Instead of punishing ourselves and others for missteps, let’s use these as opportunities to learn and grow. Being more forgiving allows us to be more peaceful and merciful to others. By offering compassion to others and ourselves, we experience the blessing and growth that comes with it. Rex Mendoza

Lord, help me develop the compassion and empathy to forgive myself and others, and to constructively learn from life’s difficulties and challenges. Saint Sabas,

## 2026-12-06 — It Doesn’T Matter

### Reflection

But do not ignore this one fact, beloved, that with the Lord one day is like a thousand years and a thousand years like one day. 13-14 – 2 “It doesn’t matter.” I often hear Second Sunday of Advent this from my husband. When I drive faster than the normal speed and try to pass other vehicles around us, he would remind me, “A few miles faster will not make a difference. We’ll get there when we get there. It doesn’t matter.” Or when I say, “Five more minutes and I’ll be ready,” he’d respond, “It doesn’t matter. I’ll wait until you’re ready.” He values time, but he doesn’t like it when things When God doesn’t seem to answer our prayers, we become impatient and act impulsively. But rushing things without careful consideration likely leads to mistakes. God isn’t bound by human calendars. His timetable is different from ours. It doesn’t matter if He grants our wishes today or 10 years later. His promise is not delayed, but unfolds according to His perfect timing. No matter how long it takes, God will keep His promises. The waiting process is His way of preparing us to embrace fully His will for our lives. Jane Gonzales-Rauch 

### Prayer

God has a divine plan and timing for each of our lives. Do you have enough faith to persevere and to keep your eyes fixed on Jesus as you wait patiently for the fulfillment of His promise? Father in heaven, grant me the grace to be steadfast in my faith and a spirit of patience as I wait on Your promises. Amen.

When they could not find a way to do this because of the crowd, they went up to the roof and lowered him on his mat through the 13-14 tiles into the middle of the crowd right in front of Jesus. – There are times when we feel like we have to do everything on our own. ## 2026-12-09 — Mary’S Lullaby

### Reflection

He who brings out the starry host one by one and calls forth each of them by name. Because of his great power and mighty strength, not one of them is missing. – Did you know that the stars can sing? Scientists who study them say that they radiate stellar sound waves that can be detected with seismic telescopes. The biggest stars make the deepest bass sounds while smaller stars have high-pitched soprano voices. If you want a sample of the kind of music the stars make, google the “music of the tilma.” The stars found on the mantle of Our Lady of Guadalupe are astronomically accurate representations of the night sky over Mexico on December 12, 1531, the day when she first appeared to Juan Diego. Inspired by the celestial pattern found on the tilma, a researcher mapped out the stars onto a musical scale which, when played on the piano or harp, results in a beautiful melody. I listen to this divine symphony to melt away my stress and restore my health of mind and spirit. In this busy season, let’s receive God’s peace and healing through this heavenly music. Marjorie Duterte

St. Juan Diego

From medical advice to healthy diets and stuff to avoid, it takes a lot of effort to find out if what we read or watch is true. Add to that the sly scamming techniques designed to trick us into giving away our private data, and what we have is an environment of distrust and skepticism. I have to admit that I become part of the problem when I unknowingly forward fake news. So becoming media literate is a skill that both young and old need today. ## 2026-12-11 — Learn To Depend

### Reflection

“I am the Lord your God, who teaches you what is best for you, who directs you in the way you should go.” – Being the eldest and only girl in our family, I was raised to be independent and responsible. At eight, when our youngest was born, my parents had a heart- to-heart talk with me and said, “If anything happens to us, take good care of your younger brothers.” Because of this, I learned to be a big sister and also a third parent to them. I learned to make decisions, solve problems, and take the initiative. But while my parents taught me to be independent, my Heavenly Father tells me to follow His lead. I understand this to mean I must depend on Him, wait on His will, and let Him show me where to go. For me, self-reliance hinders childlike dependence on God. Thinking for myself makes me impatient and prone to get ahead of His will. And initiative makes me forget to let Him lead. Despite this, the Father continues to teach me humility and surrender. Let this be a lesson coming from the Child Jesus who was obedient to the Father. Dina Pecaña

## 2026-12-13 — What’S My Light?

He was not the light, but came to testify to the light. – Years ago, I chased spotlights in the lighting industry, so to speak. I Third Sunday of Advent went after accolades, applause, and the recognition of being the main man. But age has taught me that my role isn’t to shine, but to As I lead my company, success no longer means being the brightest mind in the room. Now, I see it as empowering others’ ideas, admitting when I’m wrong, and celebrating their wins louder than my own. Their growth illuminates the path while I hold the lantern. At home, it’s not about grand gestures of love but presence. It’s listening when my wife shares her worries, even if I can’t fix them. It’s letting my kids teach me their games and tell their stories, even if I’ve heard them a hundred times. In my neighborhood, a shared umbrella in the rain or a wave to the tired tricycle driver who’s heard no kind words all day are small, quiet moments where God’s light escapes through my imperfections. I am not the flame. But in broken, ordinary acts, I can point to the One who is. Rene Espinosa

## 2026-12-14 — Less Is More

### Reflection

Then he said, “Neither will I tell you by what authority I am doing these things” – In 2017, I had a blue Christmas. I lost my job because the company where I worked suddenly closed and the management never gave our last pay. Not long after that, I lost my mother to cancer. Though I was mourning, I needed to wake up each morning and face life head-on. While Christmas was usually a time for “more,” I found myself with “less.” I skipped buying stuff. I skipped some gatherings. I skipped giving gifts. The only thing that I didn’t miss was my time with the Lord. Surprisingly, things didn’t turn out as bad as I thought they would be. In one of the homilies I heard, the priest said, “The gift that Jesus wants on His birthday is not the beautifully wrapped ones. Jesus wants to receive you—His beautifully unwrapped child without pretense or decoration.” Your Christmas may not be merry right now and it’s OK. You don’t always have to live up to expectations of the world. You just have to live with an expectancy of God fulfilling His Word. That should be exciting enough. Laarni Uy

The book of the genealogy of Jesus Christ, the son of David, the son of Abraham. – As someone who’s self-employed, it’s challenging to get new clients when existing ones end their contracts with me. So when an international client messaged me, I was excited. We did a Zoom call and I was hired right away. The pay was great too! So I did my best to serve this client. I met my deadlines, followed their instructions, and gave my all. But after only two months, they told me that my work was below their standards. It was like a bad breakup. ## 2026-12-18 — Your Silver Dollar

### Reflection

Now this is how the birth of Jesus Christ came about.– I’m a nerd. When it comes to matters of our faith, I like it when science or math confirms what I believe. So when I read about a math and astronomy professor who computed the odds of only eight Old Testament prophecies being fulfilled in Jesus, I was ecstatic. It’s 1 in 10 to the power of 17. Forget about even trying to write out that number. Instead, imagine the whole state of Texas covered two-feet deep with silver coins and a blind man picking out the coin marked with your name on his first try. And that’s just the chances of one person fulfilling eight of the prophecies. Theologians claim there are 300 to 400 in the Old Testament. And Jesus fulfilled every single one of them. So when you think that Jesus has turned a deaf ear to your prayer or that He’s abandoned you, remember that one silver dollar in a sea of coins marked just for you. Jesus came. Not randomly. Not as an afterthought. But with millenia of planning that was executed with precision. And He came for you. Rissa Singson Kawpeng

## 2026-12-19 — The Quiet Place

### Reflection

“But now you will be speechless and unable to talk until the day these things take place, because you did not believe my words, which will be fulfilled at their proper time.” – The Quiet Place isn’t a religious movie but it speaks a lot of Christian truths. The plot is about a plague of devouring creatures that descended on earth. They’re blind but their acute sense of hearing enables them to devour whatever makes noise. For humans to survive, they have to live in silence. Is this not true with our lives? To avoid the beasts of doubts, temptations, discouragement, and whatever devours our spirits, we need to return to silence. No, the angel didn’t punish Zechariah for doubting. He was led to “the quiet place.” God also led Moses through this in Mount Sinai for 40 days. Same with Elijah at Mount Horeb. Jesus went through this in the desert. Every time God wants to bless His servants with victory over sin, clarity in discerning His will, power in ministry, or grace for deeper spiritual intimacy, He leads them into silence. Why? St. Teresa of Calcutta explains, “Silence gives rise to prayer. Prayer gives rise to faith. Faith gives rise to love. And love gives rise to service.” Jon Escoto

### Reflection

“Blessed are you who believed that what was Song of Songs 2:8-14 spoken to you by the Lord would be fulfilled.” (or ) – My mentor, a man of God, prophesies great news to me. When my car kept breaking down, he said that I’d have a new car in a year’s time. True enough, a year later, I had a “new” second-hand car that I use for the ministry. Another time, we were sent away from the apartment we had been renting. He prophesied in prayer that we will own a new home next year. Again, we were able to buy our condo unit. When things seem impossible, God uses this man to speak blessings to me. It’s hard to believe his words at first, but I pray, “Let it be done to me according to this man’s word.” Mama Mary trusted God’s word through Angel Gabriel even when they seemed impossible. She believed God’s promises. She did not doubt. Her faith led to her “fiat,” her yes to God’s plan. Her faith allowed God’s plan to unfold not just for her but the salvation of the world. Faith opens the door to God’s work in our lives. Have faith, my friend! Boggs Burbos 

### Prayer

Lord, strengthen my faith as I walk through Your plan in my life. I may not see the end of the tunnel, but let me see Your Presence throughout my journey. St. Peter Canisius, priest and Doctor of the Church,

## 2026-12-22 — Better Or Blessed?

### Reflection

“From now on all generations will call me 1 blessed, for the Mighty One has done great 1 things for me.” – NIV They had a great, great, great grandfather whose family owned acres of prime land. But he gambled most of it away and eventually got in trouble with the authorities. His family could have kept their titles if they let him go to prison, but instead they gave up their financial future in exchange for his freedom. That generation had no idea they could have been tycoons had they kept their properties, but the present generation knows. “Our lives would have been so different had they made different choices,” one of the descendants said, not with sadness, but with a smile. That tragedy was only a small part of a story about relationships. About their closeness as siblings and cousins. How they remain connected after seven decades and when they come together from across the globe it’s as if no time had passed. How their own kids and grandkids value family relationships because it was modeled to them. Would things have been better if their ancestors had kept all that wealth for them to inherit? Maybe not having all the money in the world made it a bit easier for them to see just how blessed they really were. George Tolentino Gabriel 

### Saint of the Day

Saint Flavian

## 2026-12-23 — Say My Name

### Reflection

“No. He will be called John.” – My son has the same birthday as John the Baptist. We named him Lance, though, because he is the Love of ANthony and CEcil. When he started online gaming, he randomly chose the name John Tan for his gaming account and it stuck. It was funny how his college varsity teammates called him John, not knowing it wasn’t even his name. When I learned that God calls each of us by our name, I began to appreciate every given name. Regardless of our parents’ intent, our names take on a new importance and a deeper meaning that encompasses who we are and how precious and unique we are in God’s eyes. This new perspective evoked a deeper awareness of the power of my own name. Now, whenever I want a reminder of His love, I say to myself, “I love you very much, Cecilia!” All at once, I hear God’s assurance and recall His steadfastness. I feel His love wrap around me like a snuggly blanket. God knows you and loves you. Just as He named Jesus Emmanuel, He chose your name and expects great things from you! Cecil Lim 

### Prayer

Lord God of the universe, that You attend to me. (Joseph Tetlow, SJ) St. John of Kanty, priest,

## 2026-12-24 — Geriah’S Song

### Reflection

“Praise be to the Lord, the God of Israel, 2 because he has come to his people and 14, 16 redeemed them.” – Today’s Gospel is called Zechariah’s Canticle. This hits home because our son’s name, Geriah, is inspired by Zechariah’s, which means “God remembers.” Geriah came to us at a time when we thought it was already impossible for us to conceive due to several factors: advanced maternal age, three miscarriages, and reproductive immune disorders, including seronegative APAS. But God showed us that nothing is impossible with Him. I gave birth to a healthy baby boy despite the odds. As of this writing, Geriah just turned 18 months and he enjoys worshiping God with us at home and at The Feast. What a beautiful sight to see him raising his arms while babbling his made-up lyrics. His current favorite is “Have You Seen” by Feast Worship. Part of the song goes, “Have you seen how good the Lord is to us? That in a world of suffering, hope came down for us.” And that’s what we celebrate now—the coming of our Hope in a humble manger. Jesus came as a babe, and I see His face when I look at our dearest Geriah. The Lord is truly good! Maymay Salvosa Trinidad 

### Prayer

Jesus, thank You for coming into my life. You are my hope and Savior. May my life be a song of praise to You. Saint Adele,

### Saint of the Day

Saint Adele

## 2026-12-25 — Two Kinds Of

### Reflection

And the Word became flesh and made his dwelling among us, and we saw his glory. . . . – There are two kinds of Christmas Nativity of the Lord songs. There are secular ones that sing about snow, romance, gifts, and mistletoes. Then there are religious ones that sing about Jesus’ birth, the journey of the Holy Family, and the profound hope that this season brings. These songs capture the experience of solemn joy rather than mere excitement and shallow fun. I appreciate both kinds of songs. I’ve heard many beautifully written and performed religious Christmas songs. As for secular Christmas songs, let’s just say that some are better than others. But they can be quite catchy! Similarly, we can easily get caught up in all of the secular ways of celebrating Christmas. That’s not a bad thing! But let’s not forget what Christmas is really about. It’s about Jesus. It’s about Christ. The name says so itself! No matter what our schedules may be, let’s take the time today to be with the One whom we are celebrating. Mon Reyes 

### Prayer

Jesus, we love You. Thank You for the joy and hope You bring. May we come to know You in a deeper way today and every day. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Eugenia

## 2026-12-26 — Just Like Jesus

### Reflection

But Stephen, full of the Holy Spirit, looked ; 7:54-59 up to heaven and saw the glory of God, and Jesus standing at the right hand of God. Saint Stephen’s moment of death protomartyr reminds me of the Beatitude, “Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God” (). Maybe you also know of people who see Jesus at their deathbed. God gives such grace to holy souls who have allowed their trials on earth to purify them. It’s never easy to be a martyr, to die to one’s self to share God’s love or one’s faith to others. There are times when people misconstrue even the best intentions and cover their ears from listening to the truth. But this didn’t stop Stephen. His love for God was greater than the need to please men. Today, may we be filled with his courage to proclaim Jesus. It can be as simple as leading prayer at a Christmas party with unbelieving colleagues or sharing boldly what God has done for you this year at your annual family reunion. When mocked or laughed at, may we follow the example of Saint Stephen who, like Jesus, prayed for his persecutors as he was being stoned, “Lord, do not hold this sin against them.” Only the pure in mind and heart can forgive in such a heroic way. Marjorie Duterte 

### Prayer

Lord, help me to love and forgive like You do. May I have the boldness to stand up for my faith today. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Feast of Saint Stephen,

## 2026-12-27 — Faith To Obey

### Reflection

By faith, Abraham obeyed when he was ; 21:1-3 called to go out to a place that he was to (or Sirach 3:2-6, 12-14) receive as an inheritance; he went out, not knowing where he was to go. – (or ) It’s been a month since my sister (or ) died after a diagnostic surgery. I never thought things could end the way it did. I remember sitting in the car a few days later, crying my heart out to God and complaining to Him. My whole family served at The Feast for many years. I’ve prayed for many people and my prayers were often granted. But not this time. I lost confidence and I felt like a fake because of how I was feeling. I prayed for understanding, which still has not come. I was hurt and angry. Last week, a fellow preacher told me that instead of praying for understanding, maybe I could pray for acceptance. Today’s reading reminds me that God has a plan for me, to use me to preach His Word, and obey even when I don’t understand. I haven’t preached since she died, but next week Friend, I don’t know what challenges you are facing now, but I know that God’s plan is always best for us. We just need to trust and obey Him. Ped Faytaren 

### Prayer

Jesus, let not my inability to understand Your ways stand in the way of my obedience to You. St. John the Apostle,

### Saint of the Day

St. John the Apostle

## 2026-12-28 — Innocents’ Day

### Reflection

But if we are living in the light, as God is in the 1 –2:2 light, then we have fellowship with each other, and the blood of Jesus, his Son, cleanses us from all sin. – 1 NLT massacre of children caused by a man who craved for power and wanted to stop the reign of the Son of God. My parents were supposed to name me based on this celebration because I was born on this day. I’m glad they didn’t, because I don’t want to be called innocent all my life. Why? Because innocence connotes purity, sinlessness, and chastity. I know I am human and I’m not perfect. I always need God. And while I know that it’s my desire to always be pure, chaste, and sinless before God, I can only achieve this if I acknowledge and have Him in my life. Our circumstances, missed opportunities, failures, and limitations every day would often lead us to falter and to forget our greater calling. We are called to be holy as God is holy, and my birthday will always remind me of that. Our ultimate goal is to be with God living a pure, chaste, and sinless life. A life lived in full. Mike Ybiernas 

### Prayer

Lord, give me a pure, chaste, and sinless heart. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Feast of the Holy

## 2026-12-29 — Past Years’ Resolutions

### Reflection

Dear friends, I am not writing you a new 1 command but an old one, which you have had since the beginning. This old command is the message you have heard. – 1 It’s that time of the year when we’re extra energized and motivated to change. That’s why people make New Year’s resolutions. But what if you don’t need to make them? What if you just need to revisit your resolutions of past years, this time focusing on faithfulness. Focus on consistency and follow-through. Focus on finishing strong. How? More often than not, we don’ t need new knowledge; we just need to be reminded. While the world is changing fast and we sometimes feel we can’t keep up, I want you to know that old school, old insights, and old values still work. Hard work, discipline, faithfulness, humility, kindness, and love are still cool. More importantly, God still works magnificently As this year draws to a close, maybe you don’t need new tricks and resolutions up your sleeve. Go back to God. Turn to His Word. Let Him guide you. He is everlasting—the same yesterday, today, and tomorrow. Velden Lim 

### Prayer

Jesus, as this year draws to a close, give me a fresh restart. Help me finish what You started in me. Help me go “all in” this coming year. St. Thomas Becket, bishop and martyr,

## 2026-12-30 — Beautiful Life

### Reflection

Do not love the world or the things of the 1 world. If anyone loves the world, the love of the Father is not in him. – 1 A young architect received two job offers: one was a modest pay designing affordable housing while the other paid triple but required designing casinos. As he contemplated his options, he recalled his grandmother’s words: “What fills your bank account doesn’t always fill your soul.” So he chose the work that aligned with his values instead of worldly gain. Our First Reading is confrontational and can be hard to swallow. We may think we’re good Christians because we go to church on Sundays, pray, and do good works. But if we live in a worldly way the rest of the 168 hours of our week, then we need to admit that we love the world. Only by confronting the truth can we change. Our problem is we only want to see what is physically before us, what we can touch, what is now. If we want to unlove this world, then we need to see beyond the veil and look with eyes of faith at the beauty and glory that awaits us on the other side of the divide. For there we find Jesus. Joyce Roa 

### Prayer

Lord, give me the courage to face up to the truth that will set me free. Help me to turn toward You and the beautiful life that You offer. St. Sabinus,

### Saint of the Day

St. Sabinus

## 2026-12-31 — Close The Door

### Reflection

We have seen His glory, the glory of the one 1 and only Son, who came from the Father, full of grace and truth. – NIV Our retreat master, Fr. Paolo Asprer, SSP, asked, “What is that one question you’re afraid to ask God?” I had so many but top of mind was, “God, why did You allow us to lose seven digits of our hard-earned money?” After that session, I prayed and received God’s answer. “Love, I had to prune the branches that will not bear fruit in your life to save you from further harm. So you and your husband will depend on the True Vine.” It was time to let go of that bitter past, close the door of regret, and focus on the promise of the True Vine. Friends, we are about to close another year in our lives. Before you open a new page in 2027, allow God to speak to your heart. What doors do you need to close? Maybe doors of blame, shame, comparison, sin, regrets, or unforgiveness? Surrender it to the Lord. Let Him prune unfruitful branches in your life that you may bear more fruit and harvest in abundance. Love Cosio 

### Prayer

Lord, thank You for Your grace this year. I wholeheartedly let go of the things that are not fruitful in my life so I can bear more fruit in abundance. Amen.

### Saint of the Day

Saint Sylvester
